The unconfined fallback (AD-7, FQ2) and Windows (Q5, D7) are unchanged and keep their disclosed exposure.

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| `pkg/preview/confine.go::confinement` | The `readModel` field goes; a read-only folder list is added (its name is the developer's choice) |
| `pkg/preview/confine_unix.go::confinement.policy` | The read-model branch goes; read-only folders are emitted with read access only |
| `pkg/preview/programs.go::officeConversion.run` | Adds the registry folder (macOS) and the font folders of the platform, read only |
| `pkg/preview/service.go::Request` | `ReadModel` goes; `Service.Preview` builds the converter's confinement without it |
| `pkg/tools/preview_document.go::PreviewDocumentTool.Execute`, `pkg/tools/read_file_pdf.go::ReadFileTool.pdfPageRenderer`, `pkg/gateway/rest_preview_retry.go::restAPI.renderPreviewRetry` | Stop calling `sandbox.KernelPolicyForTurn` for the preview; `CallerWriteRoots` (§3.2 writer exclusion) is unchanged |
| `pkg/sandbox`, `pkg/fspolicy` | No change, amended by D8d (founder Q37 = A): one `pkg/sandbox` addition; `pkg/fspolicy` stays unchanged |

*Risks.*

| Risk | Handling |
|---|---|
| LibreOffice needs a read outside the list | Visible failure, never a silent widening; the "Adding a read" rule applies. The 2026-10-29 spike ran under the per-turn profile, so whether the list suffices is **Unknown** until T-102 runs on macOS and Linux |
| Fonts | Fonts installed only for one user account (macOS `~/Library/Fonts`, Linux `~/.local/share/fonts`) are not read; such text is shown in a substitute font. The user pages say so (§17); hands-on rows U-M1 to U-M3 check how documents look |
| The open file model loses reads it had | Intended: the converter's input is a copy in `work`; the gateway's read of the source is unchanged (FR-003) |
| A read under `<home>/system` | One folder, read only, checked like the program files, holding no user data. Security-lead reviews D8c at the gate |

*Alternatives rejected.* **Option 2**, a fixed deny on the whole call tree with the call's own folder carved out: Seatbelt has no carve-out inside a denied tree as the profile is rendered (`pkg/sandbox/seatbelt_profile.go::renderSeatbeltProfileState.renderDeniedPaths`), and on Linux `ExpandRulesExcluding` drops a rule under a denied path, so the shared sandbox code would change on both platforms, and the converter would still read the agent's other files. **Option 3**, keep D8b and document the residual: accepts that one call can read another call's files. **R2**, keep the D8b.1 copy: about 40 more lines and the old T-105, for integrity stronger than LibreOffice's own program files get. **A per-call folder outside the shared tree**: the converter would still inherit the turn's grants.

*Consequences.* Positive: one preview call cannot read another's files, on either platform, in either file model; no shared sandbox or file-policy change; D8b.1 and D8b.2 are never built; a failing deny walk cannot affect the converter. Negative: LibreOffice may need one more read (risk above); fonts installed only for one user account are not used. Neutral: no contract change. User docs change (§17, row "2026-11-08, §3.4 D8c"). Security-lead reviews D8c at the gate. macOS CI is off, so T-11, T-102 and T-105 rest on local slot-runner receipts bound to the exact commit.

*Affected components.* The six code items in the table above; tests `pkg/preview/turn_repeat_preview_office_test.go` (T-102; it builds a turn policy with `KernelPolicyForTurn` and passes `Request.ReadModel`, both of which go), the T-105 test and T-11's profile diff.

**D8d. Look-up of the folders above the converter's own folders (architect ruling, 2026-11-08; in force since founder Q37 = A, 2026-11-08; amends D8c).**

*Status:* this rule changes shared sandbox code, which is a security change, so the founder decided it: Q37 = A (2026-11-08, §21). security-lead reviews the implementation before landing.

*Context.* The developer built D8c as written (`landing/office-preview` at `8e86c0ea3`). On macOS with real LibreOffice, the registry folder now opens, but the first T-102 preview fails with a `DeploymentException` from LibreOffice's start-up. The developer's local experiments (variants v1 to v6; logs and the kernel's sandbox denial lines kept outside the repository) show:

| Variant | Added to the D8c list | Result |
|---|---|---|
| v1 | read on the folders between the registry and `system` | still the `DeploymentException`; the denials name look-ups of `/Applications` (above the program) and of the folders above the work and socket folders |
| v4 | look-up only (Seatbelt `file-read-metadata` on the exact folder, `literal`) for every folder above every listed folder, up to but not including `/` | both T-102 previews produce pages. Reading those folders' contents (`file-read-data`) is still denied many times, so LibreOffice does not need to list them |
| v5 | the same, but only for the folders above `<id>/work` | the same `DeploymentException` |
| v6 | the same, for every listed folder except `<id>/work` | no pages; LibreOffice hung until the run was stopped |

So the folders above the work folder and those above the other listed folders are both needed. With a data folder under the user's home folder they include the home folder itself, the data folder, `tmp`, `tmp/preview-calls`, `<id>`, `system`, `system/components` and `system/components/office`, plus the folders above the program, the launcher, the socket folder and the font folders. v1 also shows that the Seatbelt system preamble does not cover `/private` or `/private/tmp`, so no location avoids the need: every folder has parents.

*Decision (option A).*

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Which folders | Every folder above each folder of the converter's D8c list (component root, launcher folder, `<id>/work`, socket folder, registry folder, font folders), up to but not including `/`, computed from the real (symlink-resolved) path each rule already uses. Computed in `pkg/preview` per start, never a hand-written list |
| What it allows | Look-up only: the program may learn that the named folder exists and read its attributes (kind, permissions, owner, size, times). On macOS each folder renders as exactly `(allow file-read-metadata (literal "<folder>"))` |
| What it does not allow | Listing the folder's contents, opening or reading any file, looking up anything inside it other than the converter's own listed folders, writing or changing anything. `literal` matches that one path only, never what lies below it |
| Who gets it | Only the LibreOffice converter. The renderer and text extraction keep their D8c-era lists (MV-6); turn policies and tool policies never set it |
| macOS | Rendered as above, among the allows; the converter's policy has no deny list (D8c), so nothing can override it |
| Linux | Nothing to render. Landlock cannot restrict `stat(2)` or `chdir(2)` at all (kernel documentation, "Landlock: unprivileged access control", the warning listing actions it cannot restrict), so a confined program can already look up any path it can name; it still cannot list a folder without a grant (`pkg/sandbox/landlock_abi_rights.go` handles the read-directory right). The Linux backend MUST ignore the field: it never becomes a grant and never reaches `ExpandRulesExcluding`. Whether real LibreOffice on Linux works under the D8c list is **Unknown** until T-102 runs there. *2026-11-15/16:* neither the §3.6 variant-C entries nor the §3.5 D8e entries need a look-up rule on Linux |
| Windows | No file isolation (Q5, D7); the field is ignored |

*Why look-up only is acceptable.* The folders' paths are already known to the converter: they are in its own arguments and environment (program path, `URE_MORE_SERVICES`, `HOME` and `TMPDIR` inside `<id>/work`, `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`). What it gains is their attributes. The useful part of that is timing: a folder's modification time (and, on some file systems, its link count) changes when an entry inside it is added or removed. So a converter under attacker control can tell *when* something changed in, for example, the shared preview-call folder or the data folder, and roughly how many sub-folders it has, but not *what* changed, no names and no content. On Linux the kernel feature gives every confined program this much already, so D8d brings macOS to the level Linux has always had. Option B (full read on the parents) would add listing, that is the names of the user's home-folder entries, the data folder's entries and other preview calls, which v4 shows LibreOffice does not need.

*`pkg/sandbox` change (amends D8c's "No change" row for `pkg/sandbox`; `pkg/fspolicy` stays unchanged).*

| Item | Change |
|---|---|
| `pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go::SandboxPolicy` | One new field holding look-up-only folders (name is the developer's), documented with the meaning above |
| `pkg/sandbox/seatbelt_profile.go` | Renders each entry as exactly one `(allow file-read-metadata (literal "<folder>"))` line, quoted as other paths are. An entry that is not an absolute, cleaned path aborts the render (fail closed, the spawn does not happen). Look-up entries do not count as an allow for the existing "no allows" refusal |
| `pkg/sandbox/seatbelt_cache.go::seatbeltPolicyCacheKey` | Hashes the whole policy value (`%#v`), so the new field is part of the key without a code change; T-106 proves it |
| Linux backend | Ignores the field (see the Linux row) |
| `pkg/preview` | `confinement` gains the look-up list; `confinement.policy` copies it; `programs.go::officeConversion.run` computes it for the converter only |

*Test seam (ruling on the developer's choice).* **Accepted:** `pkg/preview/confine.go::testHookConverterStart` with `spawnHooks.observedBy`, instead of an exported `RenderedProfile()` accessor on the Seatbelt backend that the T-11 RED test asked for. Reasons: (1) the hook records the command line of this very start after the wrap, and `pkg/sandbox/backend_darwin_seatbelt.go::SeatbeltBackend.ApplyToCmd` passes the profile inline (`sandbox-exec -p <profile>`), so the oracle is the exact profile the start used; (2) a backend-wide "last rendered profile" is ambiguous when up to two renderer starts and one converter start run (`pkg/preview/service.go`: `maxRendererRuns = 2`, `maxOfficeRuns = 1`); the developer's own diagnostic, which kept the last profile written, captured the renderer's profile instead of LibreOffice's, which is exactly this trap; (3) no exported test-only accessor is added to a security package. Conditions: the variable stays unexported and is set only by tests in package `preview`; a test that sets it does not run in parallel and resets it with `t.Cleanup`; a test that expects a confined start fails (never skips) when the hook was called zero times; T-11 reads the profile from the captured `-p` argument. qa-lead moves the T-11 and T-105 (e) tests onto this seam; that is a test-side change.

*Observed, not added.* With v4, LibreOffice produced pages although the sandbox still denied it: locale data under `/usr/share/locale`, ICU data under `/usr/share/icu`, the time-zone database, the global preferences file, a font cache outside the font folders, and several Mach services. None of these is added. Whether their absence changes page content (for example a date formula recalculated on load using a different time zone, or a different sort order) is **Unknown**; hands-on row U-M3 looks at a workbook. Any later read follows D8c "Adding a read"; Mach services stay at 2 (MV-3).

*Risks.*

| Risk | Handling |
|---|---|
| A rendering slip widens access (`subpath` instead of `literal`, or `file-read*` instead of `file-read-metadata`) | T-106 checks the exact rendered text; security-lead reviews the `pkg/sandbox` change at the gate |
| A look-up entry turns into a grant on Linux | T-106 checks that a policy with look-up entries gives Linux exactly the rules it gives without them |
| Folders computed from unresolved paths | LibreOffice fails visibly (no widening); T-102 runs from a temporary folder reached through a symlink, so a mistake shows |
| A later LibreOffice needs more | Visible failure; T-102 on each update |
| Timing information through folder attributes | Accepted as stated above; disclosed on the user page |

*Alternatives rejected.* **B**, reuse the existing symlinked-ancestor rule (`file-read*` on each parent, as `pkg/sandbox/seatbelt_profile.go` emits for symlinked ancestors): it also allows listing those folders, which exposes entry names of the user's home folder, the data folder and other preview calls, and v4 shows listing is not needed. **C**, move the work folder and registry elsewhere: brings back the withdrawn registry copy, and does not remove the need, because any location has parents and the preamble does not cover even `/private/tmp` (v1).

*Consequences.* Positive: Office preview works on macOS under D8c's separation, with no listing and no file reads outside the list. Negative: one addition to shared sandbox code; the converter can see when the folders above its own change. Neutral: no contract change; the user page sentence changes (§17, row "2026-11-08, §3.4 D8d").

*Affected components.* `pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go`, `pkg/sandbox/seatbelt_profile.go`, the Linux backend's rule building (unchanged behaviour, proven by T-106), `pkg/preview/confine.go`, `confine_unix.go`, `programs.go`; tests T-11, T-102, T-106.

**D9. Refusal text on settings routes (founder Q24 = A).** New helper in `pkg/gateway`, same shape as `rest_channels_outbound_guard.go::writeOutboundProtected`:

`writeRequestRefusal(w http.ResponseWriter, err error) bool` answers a `*requestRefusalError` with 400 and its text, and reports whether it did.

Each site calls it directly before its generic failure, after any `configRefreshError` branch. Logs, rollback and 500 bodies stay as they are. `rest_sandbox_config.go::writeSandboxSaveError` calls it too, so one mapping exists. `rest_agents.go::withToolPolicyCoverageGuard`, `rest_config.go::updateConfig` and `rest_integrations_remove_key.go::handleSearchKeyRemoval` already keep the refusal and do not change.

Every route below saves through the shared config writer, which can return the refusal: an entry on disk that the published configuration lacks is refused on any save (`rest_config.go::writeRefusableConfigJSONLocked`, `preview_program_access.go::refuseNewProgramAccess`). So a declared 400 is needed on all 20. Sixteen declare it; four do not.

| # | Rule |

Security notes for security-lead (founder Q26: the sign-in routes need security-lead's review at the gate). (a) Any user with valid credentials, not only an admin, can see the text on rows 21 to 23. It names one config entry and the program folder and holds no credential. A generic text for non-admins was rejected because it would hide the reason again, against Q26. (b) A refused sign-out leaves the server-side session valid. This is true today for any write failure there, and the screen signs out locally and only logs the failure. Recorded in §3.3, not changed here.

**Contract.** `backend-lead` adds `'400'` (the existing `400BadRequest` response) to D9 routes 6, 8, 11 and 13 and D9b route 22 in `contracts/openapi.yaml`, adds the code `program_access_refused` to `contracts/components/schemas/ChannelOutboundRejection.yaml` (D9b route 34), and regenerates with `scripts/gen-contracts.sh`, in the same commit as D9 and D9b and before their handler changes (Hard Constraint 8). D1 to D8 change no contract: `owner.json` crosses no gateway/SPA boundary.

**Comments that change with this code.** `pkg/preview/scratch.go::socketDirPattern` (today states a bound security review rejected), `::call` (`root` field), `::ownerRecord`, `::scratchRoot`, `::Recover` (old path); `pkg/preview/launcher/launcher.go` package comment: Linux "address-space (or data)" (§3.1 D1 chose data only), and Windows "or the gateway's death, closes the job" unless T-99 proves it true (§3.3).

**Consequences.**

*Positive.* Recovery acts only on what the gateway wrote, where confinement exists. One move closes the two crash-recovery findings and the publish gap. FR-050 keeps its text. A refused save names its reason on all 20 routes.

*Negative.* Tests that assume the old path or `PGID` change (T-92 onward; each keeps its strength, and the identity assertion in `pkg/preview/retry_office_test.go` is replaced by T-94, never deleted). macOS CI is off (`.github/workflows/cross-platform.yml`), so macOS rows are proven only by local runs bound to the exact commit. A socket folder created just before a crash, before its publish, is left behind empty; nothing has run by then, and the window is accepted.

*Neutral.* User documentation: none needed for D1 to D7, because no user-visible behaviour changes and `docs/documents.md`, `docs/security.md` and `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md` do not describe the scratch layout or recovery. D7's limit is already covered by those pages' Windows and unconfined exposure text (§17). D8 fixes a failure against what the pages already promise. D9 needs none either: `docs/documents.md` ("What previews need") already promises the exact text on a save. D9b changes what sign-in, sign-out, password change, onboarding and the starting-messages screen show, so §17 has a 2026-11-07 row for it. docs-verifier confirms all of these at the §17 audit.

**Alternatives considered.**

| Option | Why rejected |
|---|---|
| Keep the record in scratch and write it through `call.root` | Satisfies FR-050 and leaves both crash-recovery findings open: the converter can still rewrite it |
| Sign the record | The key must survive a crash, so it has the same reach as the record; more code, no new boundary |
| Move the record under `system/` | Stronger against agent shells, but splits one call across two trees and needs a second sweep. Fallback if security-lead disagrees |
| Keep root `tmp/preview` | New code would trust a file an old converter could write |
| Recovery only deletes folders on Windows and the fallback (Q22 B) | The Windows launcher may need that stop (§3.3) |
| Windows file confinement (Q22 C) | A separate large feature; reopens Q5 |
| Render the sandbox profile once per call (Q23 C) | Touches `pkg/sandbox`; the guard closes the path at far lower cost |
| Rename `writeSandboxSaveError` and swap it into each site | Sites use different failure bodies and logs; a helper that only handles the refusal leaves them untouched |

**Affected components.** `pkg/preview/scratch.go`, `pkg/preview/scratch_anchor.go`, `pkg/preview/programs.go`, `pkg/preview/launcher/launcher.go` (comments); for D8, `pkg/preview/confine_unix.go` or the code T-102 points to; the `pkg/gateway` handlers in the D9 table, `rest_channels_configure_apply.go`, `rest_sandbox_config.go`, `contracts/openapi.yaml` and the generated types. Tests: `pkg/preview/scratch_test.go`, `pkg/preview/retry_office_test.go`, `pkg/preview/scratch_recovery_socketdir_office_test.go`, `pkg/gateway/preview_publish_barrier_office_test.go`.

### 3.5 Dated amendment 2026-11-15 — Linux converter private folder + converter-only system-call filter (architect; DESIGN-v3; founder Q-A B, Q-B A, Q-C B, V2-F1…F4 = B, flag 2 = B, flag 3 = A)

**Deferred to #1856 — not part of this landing (founder split, 2026-11-16, §3.6).** Everything below is the design for issue #1856 (target 2026-11-27) and is kept unchanged as its specification. For this landing the Linux converter follows §3.6 variant C.

Status: decided 2026-11-15 (architect amendment applying the founder-approved design). Founder answers: Q76 A (2026-11-10), Q77 A, Q78 C, Q79 A, Q80 A, Q-A B, Q-B A, Q-C B, V2-F1…V2-F4 B, flag 2 B, flag 3 A (§1). Adds D8e below, FR-057…FR-060, B-71…B-76, T-107…T-113 and U-L4. Amends AD-7 (and its environment table), AD-8, FR-009, MV-2, MV-8, §3.4 D2, D3, D4, D8c and D8d, T-97, T-102, §11, §17, §18, §19. Sources, kept outside the repository in the office-preview gate scratch area (`q76-strace/`): DESIGN-v3 (§8 is the change list; §1 decisions; §6 user-guide text; §7 tests; §9 tracked items), DESIGN-v2 (§10 is the base delta v3 amends; v3 corrects six v2 statements, K1–K6, and only the corrected text is carried here) and security-lead's SECURITY-REVIEW-v2. Code basis: `landing/office-preview` at `e7e8be00a`; `pkg/preview` is unchanged there since `226edef1c`, the commit the design and review read. Security wording is class-level (public repository).

*Corrections made in place (architect, 2026-11-15).*

| # | Spec said | Wrong because | Correct is |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | AD-7: on Linux, "TCP bind/connect denied where Landlock supports it (not UDP — documented)" for every preview program | `pkg/preview/confine_linux.go::hooksFor` adds a bind and connect allow rule for port 0, which the kernel reads as "any port the system picks", so TCP bind is not denied; and for the converter FR-059 now refuses IPv4/IPv6 socket creation itself | Converter: no IPv4/IPv6 socket of its own and no port allow rule. PDF renderer and text extractor: existing rules, port-0 rule included, kept by founder Q-C B; the gap is tracked as T3 |
| 2 | AD-7 environment table, FR-009, MV-2: the Linux converter gets the four entries and `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` is macOS only | D8e steers LibreOffice on Linux through `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` | Linux: the four entries plus `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`, absent only where D8e.2 leaves no private folder (no active kernel sandbox, folder not made). The macOS and Windows rows and FR-009 now name "the four" explicitly, because "the four Linux entries" no longer means four |
| 3 | AD-7: "writes only scratch and (macOS) the socket folder"; AD-8: "(macOS socket folder separate)" | Linux now has a per-call socket folder | Socket folder on macOS and, since D8e, Linux |
| 4 | §3.4 D8c table: socket folder on Linux "none"; no `/dev/urandom` row | D8e | Linux: `/tmp/o-<8 hex>` read and write (Q-B A); `/dev/urandom` read only, converter only (Q77 A); no rule ever names `/tmp` itself or `/var/tmp` |
| 5 | §3.4 D4: socket-folder identity not recorded, "the worst result is one failed preview" | A kept record can name a folder that was already removed and whose name was later reused; recovery could then empty the reused folder (V2-F3) | Known limit, tracked as T2 |
| 6 | T-97: "macOS only (no other platform has a socket folder)" | Linux has one since D8e | macOS and Linux |
| 7 | T-102: write rules are `{<id>/work}` on Linux | D8e adds the private folder | `{<id>/work, the private folder}` on Linux, as on macOS |
| 8 | §18 W1: RED owns T-92…T-106 | New rows | T-92…T-113; W2b also builds D8e |

**D8e. LibreOffice's control socket on Linux: a private folder per conversion and a converter-only system-call filter (architect amendment, 2026-11-15; amends AD-7, AD-8, D2, D3, D4, D8c, D8d).**

*Context.* Office preview fails on Linux at LibreOffice's first start (T-102 and the real-program tests; `soffice.bin` exit 1). A system-call trace on the Linux CI runner (Landlock ABI 8; runs 42793021593, 42793839583, 42795796305; logs outside the repository) found three refusals in this order: creating the control socket `OSL_PIPE_[<uid>_]SingleOfficeIPC_<hash>` in `/tmp` (exit 1); reading `/dev/urandom` (exit 134); removing that socket after the exit-81 first start (§3.1 D3), so the second start fails. LibreOffice cannot turn the control socket off. It picks the socket's folder by asking whether `/tmp`, then `/var/tmp`, is writable, and reads `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` (from `-env:` arguments, then the environment) only when neither is; it never reads `TMPDIR`. Landlock does not answer that question, so on Linux the per-call private folder macOS already uses (`pkg/preview/scratch.go::call.makeSocketDir`, `/private/tmp/o-<8 hex>`) can never be chosen. The founder chose a private folder per conversion (Q78 C) over a create-socket-only right on the shared `/tmp` (the first Q76 design), over a socket connectable by every local account and over account-list reads; read-only `/dev/urandom` (Q77 A); no listening on any port, with a test (Q79 A); `/tmp` only, never `/var/tmp` (Q80 A). The converter is treated as compromised (founder Q18 = A, §3.4).

*D8e.1 Decision.*

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Private folder | Per call, Omnipus (outside the sandbox) checks that `/tmp` is a real folder (not a link), owned by root, with the sticky bit set; then `mkdir /tmp/o-<8 random hex>` with mode 0700, which fails if the name exists, so a squatted name causes a failure, never a takeover, and is never cleaned. Parent is always `/tmp`, never `/var/tmp` or a configured temporary folder (Q80 A). Right after `mkdir`, and again before the exit-81 second start, Omnipus checks **without following links** that the folder is a real folder owned by its own account, with permissions exactly 0700 and no access-control list granting others. This catches a bad starting state (an unusual creation mask, an inherited access list); it does not stop a converter taken over by a document from changing the folder later (T1). Order stays `mkdir` → record `socket_dir` in `owner.json` (§3.4 D2) → start (V2-F4 accepted). The folder is passed as `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`. The socket path stays far below the 108-byte socket-name limit (about 80 bytes, Inferred from the name format) |
| `socketDirPattern` | `pkg/preview/scratch.go::socketDirPattern` becomes per platform: Linux `^/tmp/o-[0-9a-f]{8}$`, macOS unchanged `^/private/tmp/o-[0-9a-f]{8}$`. A guard against a damaged record only, never a discovery pattern: Omnipus never lists `/tmp` |
| Converter rules (Linux) | Beyond the §3.4 D8c list, only: read and write on its own private folder (founder Q-B A: LibreOffice may create and delete inside it, the macOS rule) and read on `/dev/urandom` (Q77 A; the backend keeps only the file-read right for a non-folder, `pkg/sandbox/sandbox_linux.go::addLandlockPathRule`, so nothing else under `/dev`). No rule on `/tmp` itself, none on `/var/tmp`, no port-0 or other port allow rule. Set only in `pkg/preview/programs.go::officeConversion.run`; renderer, extractor, turn and tool policies never get them |
| Converter filter | A second, converter-only seccomp filter (Linux system-call filter), installed by the launcher (`run-limited`) after the limits and the parent-death signal, after "no new privileges" is set, for all threads (TSYNC), before the go-byte and `exec`. Filters cannot be removed, so LibreOffice and everything it starts inherit it; it stacks on the gateway's own filter and the kernel applies the strictest answer. Rules F-1…F-6 below. Lives in `pkg/sandbox` beside `seccomp_linux.go::assembleBPFMode`, sharing its architecture and x32 preamble; it must not touch `processSeccompInstalled` (the gateway's boot-filter guard) |
| Per-program switch (Q-C B) | One new boolean on `pkg/preview/confine.go::confinement`, set only by the Office converter (name: developer's choice). `pkg/preview/confine_linux.go::hooksFor` adds today's port-0 bind/connect rules **unless** it is set, so the renderer and extractor keep exactly today's rules. One new launcher limit in `pkg/preview/launcher/launcher.go::Limits`: filter mode `off` / `required` / `best-effort`, set only in `officeConversion.run` (D8e.2); every other caller leaves `off`. The `hooksFor` comment is rewritten to the truth: with Landlock 4+ an empty list denies every TCP bind/connect (`pkg/sandbox/sandbox_linux.go::effectiveHandledAccessNet`); the port-0 rule permits binding a port the system picks and is kept for the renderer and extractor by founder decision Q-C B |
| macOS, Windows | The boolean is ignored; a non-`off` filter mode makes the non-Linux launcher exit with its usage error (fail closed). The macOS socket-folder route is otherwise unchanged; the D8e.3 cleanup corrections apply there too |
| Failure texts | `conversion failed: LibreOffice's private folder could not be created in /tmp` and `conversion failed: this Linux system does not allow the system-call filter Office preview needs`. Log warnings, each once per gateway run: `office preview: private folder unavailable` and `office preview: system-call filter unavailable` |

Filter rules (exact content):

| Rule | Match | Answer | Purpose | Security boundary? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F-1 | `access` (where the architecture has it) with the write bit (`W_OK`, 2) set in argument 2 | `EACCES` | Steer LibreOffice to `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` | No — a steering hint. If it misses, LibreOffice tries `/tmp`, Landlock refuses the socket (no `/tmp` right) and the preview fails visibly |
| F-2 | `faccessat`, `faccessat2` with `W_OK` set in argument 3 | `EACCES` | Same (arm64 and riscv64 have no `access` call) | No |
| F-3 | `socket` with domain `AF_INET` (2) or `AF_INET6` (10), any type (TCP, UDP, raw) | `EACCES` | The converter cannot create an IP socket itself (Q79 A as narrowed by V2-F2 B) | Yes |
| F-4 | `socketcall` where the architecture has it | `EPERM` | Closes the multiplexed route around F-3 | Yes |
| F-5 | `io_uring_setup` | `EPERM` | io_uring can create, bind and listen on sockets without the calls F-3 sees; LibreOffice does not use it | Yes |
| F-6 | Any call from a non-native architecture and (amd64) any x32 call | `EPERM` | Same guard as the gateway filter, so F-3…F-5 cannot be bypassed through another numbering table | Yes |
| — | everything else | allow | | |

Argument checks compare the low 32 bits only (the kernel truncates these `int` arguments the same way, so high bits cannot slip a value past). `listen` is not filtered: LibreOffice must listen on its Unix socket, and a filter cannot tell socket families apart on `listen`; F-3 removes every IP socket instead.

*D8e.2 Filter mode and installation (Q-A B, flag 2 B).* The gateway decides whether the kernel sandbox is active for the converter **before** creating the folder, with the check `hooksFor` already makes (`sandbox.CurrentLinuxBackend().EnforcingChildren()`).

| Situation (Linux) | Filter mode the gateway asks the launcher for | If it fails |
|---|---|---|
| Kernel sandbox active for the converter | **required** | `/tmp` check, folder creation or recording, or the D8e.1 folder check (at creation or before the second start) fails → the call ends with the folder text, nothing started. Filter installation fails → the launcher exits with a new filter-failure code before the go-byte; LibreOffice never starts; the preview fails with the filter text. Nothing is tried in `/var/tmp` |
| Kernel sandbox not active (older kernel, sandbox off, permissive mode), folder made | **best-effort** | Installation fails → the launcher writes one line to its error output and starts LibreOffice without the filter; the gateway logs `office preview: system-call filter unavailable` once per gateway run; LibreOffice then picks the shared `/tmp`, as today; the result already says `converted without sandbox: <reason>` (AD-7) |
| Kernel sandbox not active, filter installs | best-effort | Not a failure: private folder and network block apply; there is still no file isolation (existing disclosure) |
| Kernel sandbox not active, **private folder cannot be made** (`/tmp` check fails, `mkdir` fails, recording fails, or the D8e.1 folder check fails at creation) | **off** (no filter) | Not a failure: Omnipus removes any folder it just made by its exact name; no `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` is set; the gateway logs `office preview: private folder unavailable` once per gateway run; LibreOffice runs as today in the shared `/tmp`, without the network block; the result carries `converted without sandbox: <reason>`. The check before the second start is skipped here: without a kernel sandbox the converter has no file boundary for it to protect |

Why **off** and not best-effort in the last row: with the "not writable" answer active and no `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`, LibreOffice would place its socket in the root folder, which an ordinary account cannot create, so the preview would fail; the network block is therefore also absent there, which matches today's behaviour the founder chose. This is not a silent retry: one start, a log line, the existing per-result disclosure and the user-guide paragraph (§17).

Installation contract (implementation acceptance conditions, from security-lead's review):

| Rule | Detail |
|---|---|
| Success means return value 0 | With the all-threads flag (TSYNC) the kernel can return a positive thread number to report that synchronising failed; that is a failure, not success (`seccomp(2)`) |
| "No new privileges" first | Failure to set it = installation failure |
| Always enforcing | The converter filter answers with errors, never "log only", even when the gateway's own filter runs in permissive (log-only) mode |
| Office only | Selected by the Office converter's start only; other launcher users (renderer, extractor) never get it |
| Early exit is still a clear failure | If the launcher exits with the filter-failure code before the gateway has recorded its identity or sent the go-byte, the gateway still reports the filter text, not a generic start failure |

*D8e.3 Cleanup.* The converter never cleans `/tmp`. Omnipus, outside the sandbox, removes the recorded folder by its exact name with `os.RemoveAll` (it unlinks a link inside the folder rather than following it; in the sticky `/tmp` only the owner or root can rename the folder itself). Promise: **Omnipus removes the preview's private folder when the preview ends, and after a crash when Omnipus starts again, in almost all cases.**

| Event | Left behind | Removed by |
|---|---|---|
| Normal exit, both starts | Nothing in `/tmp` itself; LibreOffice removes its own socket (Q-B A) | `call.finish` removes `/tmp/o-<id>` when the call returns |
| Between start 1 (exit 81) and start 2 | LibreOffice removed its socket at exit 81; if not, start 2's own stale-socket handling removes it | — |
| Stop, 120 s deadline, memory kill, LibreOffice crash | Socket inside the private folder | `call.finish` (deferred in `pkg/preview/service.go::Service.Preview`) |
| Gateway crash mid-conversion | Folder and socket | Next start: `recoverOne` → `removeRecordedSocketDir` from `owner.json` `socket_dir`, after the launcher check |
| Removing the folder fails | — | Stop **before** `work`: keep `work`, the call folder and the record, warn without the path; the next recovery retries the whole sequence (`call.finish`, `removeRecordedSocketDir` and `recoverOne` report whether removal succeeded). A recorded path outside the pattern: keep the record, warn without the path |

| Known limit | Status |
|---|---|
| The gateway dies after creating the folder but before recording it (or recording fails and the rollback removal also fails): the empty folder stays in `/tmp`; Omnipus never scans `/tmp` to find it | **Accepted** (V2-F4 B). No document or socket has entered the folder at that point |
| A kept record can name a folder that was already removed and whose name was later reused; recovery could then empty the reused folder | **Tracked, T2** (V2-F3 B). Low probability (random 8-hex name; the removal must have succeeded while record retirement failed), but possible |
| Removing the name does not end connections already open; processes that escaped the process group are out of scope | Unchanged, already disclosed |

*What it guarantees (Linux, kernel sandbox active, filter installed).*

| Operation | Before (`226edef1c`) | After |
|---|---|---|
| Create, delete or rename anything in `/tmp` itself | Refused (that was the bug) | Refused (no rule on `/tmp`) |
| Touch `/var/tmp` | Refused | Refused; never named in any rule |
| Create its socket in `/tmp/o-<id>` | — | Allowed; also create and delete other entries there (Q-B A) |
| Other local accounts connect to the socket | n/a (socket never created) | Refused **while the converter leaves its folder as Omnipus created it** (an honest converter), including accounts sharing the service account's primary or a supplementary group; root and the service account itself excepted. A converter taken over by a document can widen the folder's permissions (T1) |
| Read `/dev/urandom` | Refused (exit 134) | Read only |
| Create an IPv4/IPv6 socket itself (TCP, UDP, raw) | TCP connect refused (Landlock 4+), TCP bind on a port the system picks allowed, UDP allowed | Refused on every kernel that has seccomp, also kernels whose Landlock has no TCP control |
| Talk to other local programs | Allowed | Still allowed; such a program could make network connections for the converter (accepted narrower promise, V2-F2 B) |

*Risks.*

| Risk | Handling |
|---|---|
| **Accepted (founder Q-A B):** without an active kernel sandbox and without the filter, the shared-`/tmp` socket and full network access of today return | Recorded as accepted risk; disclosed (§17). With an active kernel sandbox today's behaviour is not available (the converter has no `/tmp` right), so those systems fail with the clear text |
| **Accepted (founder flag 2 B):** without an active kernel sandbox, a private folder that cannot be made leaves the converter as today, without the network block | Accepted; one log warning per gateway run plus the existing disclosure |
| **Accepted (founder Q-B A):** where `/tmp` is held in memory, a hostile document can fill memory there for up to 120 s per conversion; the per-file size limit and the deadline do not bound the total | Accepted; not a proven small amount; stated on the user page |
| **Accepted narrower promise (founder V2-F2 B):** the converter cannot open network connections itself; it can still talk to local programs, which could do so for it. Q79 A's listening test now proves only that the converter cannot open a listening socket itself | Accepted; T-109 asserts only the converter's own sockets; received sockets and local relays are not asserted |
| **Accepted (founder V2-F4 B):** a crash between folder creation and recording leaves an empty folder in `/tmp` | Accepted; D8e.3 known limit |
| **Tracked T1 (V2-F1, High; founder B):** "Document preview: keep a converter's private socket folder private for the whole conversion" | A sandboxed child running under the service account can currently change the permissions of folders it owns; the preview's private folder needs protection that lasts for the child's lifetime. Not designed here; the user guide carries the caveat. Its tests are written with the fix |
| **Tracked T2 (V2-F3; founder B):** "Document preview: recovery should confirm a temporary folder's identity before removing it" | Recovery currently trusts a recorded folder name; it should confirm it is still the same folder the call created. Its tests are written with the fix |
| **Tracked T3 (founder flag 3 A):** "Document preview: apply the network restriction to every preview program" | The network restriction added for the Office converter should cover the PDF renderer and text extractor too. **Not described in the public user guide until fixed** (§17 scope rule) |
| The "not writable" answer reaches every write-check LibreOffice makes, not only `/tmp`: the input document (opened read-only), NSS's certificate store (read-only), fontconfig caches (no cache saved; each start rescans fonts, time impact **Unknown**); anything a Word or PowerPoint document triggers is **Unknown** | T-107 converts a workbook, a Word document and a presentation, checks rendered content, and records both start durations against the 120 s limit |
| A later `pkg/sandbox` handles Landlock's Unix-connect right (LibreOffice's shutdown self-connect is a Unix-socket connect to its own pipe; F-3 does not touch Unix sockets) | Keep T-107 (real two-start conversion) as the regression guard; decide any grant from evidence when that day comes (the kernel keeps sockets made inside the same Landlock domain reachable) |
| LibreOffice's print-service probe (port 631) now fails at `socket()` instead of `connect()` | Expected harmless (it already handles the refusal); T-107 proves it |
| Kernels or runtimes that refuse seccomp filters | Inferred rare (Omnipus's own sandbox already depends on them); behaviour per D8e.2 |

*Alternatives rejected.* **Per-call `TMPDIR`:** LibreOffice never reads it for the socket. **Private mount namespace** (bind a private folder over `/tmp`): the gateway's own filter refuses `mount` for every child and a Landlock-restricted launcher cannot mount; Ubuntu 24.04 and later also restrict unprivileged user namespaces by default; not simple. **Truly read-only `/tmp` for the converter:** needs the same namespace. **Path-precise interception of `access()`** (seccomp user notification with a supervisor): a new long-lived supervisor per call for a steering hint. **The first Q76 design** (create-socket-only right on the shared `/tmp`, computed socket names, a new `pkg/sandbox` access bit): replaced by Q78 C; it left a socket connectable by every local account. **Account-list reads:** rejected (Q78 not B). **Q-A A** (fail on every Linux system where the filter cannot be installed): recommended by architect and security-lead; the founder chose B. **Q-B B** (socket-only, Omnipus empties the folder between starts): needs a new Linux-only sandbox right and more code. **Q-C A** (network block for all three programs): the founder chose B; tracked as T3.

*Consequences.* Positive: an honest converter's control channel is no longer reachable by other local accounts; no right on `/tmp`, nothing on `/var/tmp`, no account-list reads, no socket-name calculation, no new `pkg/sandbox` access bit; the converter cannot create an IP socket itself on every Linux kernel with seccomp, including kernels whose Landlock has no TCP control (also closes the converter's UDP gap); one mechanism for macOS and Linux (private folder + `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` + exact-name removal), and the record-retention fix benefits macOS too. Negative: a second seccomp filter exists (new code in a security focus area; security-lead reviews); the "not writable" answer has side effects (risks); the converter can create files in its private folder (Q-B memory risk); on systems without an active kernel sandbox the protection is best-effort (Q-A B, flag 2 B); T1, T2 and T3 stay open. Neutral: no wire-contract change (`owner.json` crosses no gateway/SPA boundary); Windows unchanged; footprint one small filter program per conversion; graceful degradation keeps the existing `converted without sandbox` disclosure; user page change (§17, row "2026-11-15, §3.5 D8e").

*Affected components.* `pkg/sandbox/seccomp_linux.go` (new converter filter builder and installer) plus a non-Linux stub; `pkg/preview/launcher/launcher.go` (`Limits` filter mode, new exit code); `pkg/preview/launcher/child_unix.go::Main` (install on Linux; the non-Linux launcher refuses a non-`off` mode); `pkg/preview/programs.go::officeConversion.run` (Linux folder, `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`, `/dev/urandom`, filter mode, exit-code mapping, the two failure texts); `pkg/preview/confine.go::confinement` (Office boolean); `pkg/preview/confine_linux.go::hooksFor` (port-0 rules unless Office; comment); `pkg/preview/scratch.go` (`makeSocketDir`, `socketDirPattern`, `call.finish`, `removeRecordedSocketDir`, `recoverOne`); `scripts/office-preview-real/real-tests.txt`; `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`. Owner: backend-lead; security-lead reviews (`pkg/sandbox`); qa-lead RED (T-107…T-113). Before editing, backend-lead runs GitNexus impact on `hooksFor`, `call.finish`, `recoverOne` and `launcher.Limits` and lists the readers in the gate brief (not run for this amendment).

### 3.6 Dated amendment 2026-11-16 — functional Linux landing: variant C now, §3.5 D8e deferred to #1856 (architect; founder split and Q1 A, 2026-11-16)

Status: decided 2026-11-16 (founder, in the terminal, relayed by team-lead). Adds FR-061, FR-062, B-77, B-78, T-114…T-118 and U-L5. Marks §3.5 D8e, FR-057…FR-060, B-71…B-76, T-107…T-113 and U-L4 **deferred to #1856 — not part of this landing**; their text is kept as the design for #1856. Amends, for this landing, AD-7 (and its environment table), AD-8, FR-009, MV-2, MV-8, §3.4 D2, D3, D8c and D8d, T-97, T-102, §11, §17, §18, §19 and §21. Baseline: `landing/office-preview` at `93c601f59`. Measured source: the Linux converter diagnostic report (REPORT.md in the office-preview gate scratch area, `q76-strace/`, outside the repository; runs 42793839583 and 42795796305, converter rules re-created outside Omnipus).

*Context.* §3.5 D8e (a private folder per conversion plus a converter-only system-call filter) is new code in a security focus area, not yet built, with three tracked follow-ups (T1–T3). Until something is built, Office preview fails on Linux at LibreOffice's first start. The diagnostic measured which rights the converter needs, with the converter's Landlock rules re-created outside Omnipus (not reproduced there: the launcher's limits, seccomp and the test harness's outer layer):

| Variant | Rights added to today's converter rules | Measured outcome |
|---|---|---|
| control | none | exit 1 (today's failure: socket in `/tmp` refused) |
| sock | make-socket on `/tmp` | exit 134 (`/dev/urandom` refused) |
| A | make-socket on `/tmp`, read `/dev/urandom` | start 2 exit 1: start 1 could not remove its socket |
| B | A + remove-file on `/tmp` | start 2 exit 0, PDF produced, socket removed |
| **C** | **B + read `/etc/passwd` and `/etc/nsswitch.conf`** | **PDF produced; socket named with the account number, not set to mode 0777** |

Without the account lookup LibreOffice sets its socket to mode 0777, so any local account can connect to the converter's control channel for the whole conversion; variant C avoids that. Landlock rules are hierarchical: a right on `/tmp` covers everything below it. `pkg/sandbox` path rules know only read, write and execute (`pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go::AccessRead`, `AccessWrite`, `AccessExecute`), and write maps to every write-class Landlock right (`pkg/sandbox/sandbox_linux.go::LinuxBackend.accessToLandlockRights`), so variant C cannot be expressed with today's values without granting far more.

*Decision.*

| # | Rule | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| D1 | Split (founder, 2026-11-16) | Office preview lands as soon as it works end to end. Hardening and edge cases move to epic #1855. The §3.5 D8e Linux converter isolation becomes issue #1856 (target 2026-11-27); everything §3.5 adds is deferred to #1856 and is not part of this landing |
| D2 | Variant C (founder Q1 A) | For this landing the Linux converter gets, beyond the §3.4 D8c list, exactly: make-socket and remove-file beneath `/tmp`, and read (file read only) on `/dev/urandom`, `/etc/passwd` and `/etc/nsswitch.conf` (FR-061). Nothing on `/var/tmp`, no `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`, no system-call filter, no change to the port rules: the converter, the PDF renderer and the text extractor keep today's Linux network rules (`pkg/preview/confine_linux.go::hooksFor`, port-0 rule included). Set only in the Linux branch of `pkg/preview/programs.go::officeConversion.run`; renderer, extractor, turn and tool policies never get these rights. macOS and Windows unchanged. *2026-11-19, §3.7 BR-1:* still true of the converter's own rules; separately, the gateway's confined Linux boot set carries three reads (`/etc/passwd`, `/usr/share/fonts`, `/usr/local/share/fonts`), which are not converter rights |
| D3 | `pkg/sandbox` shape | One new path-rule access value (name: developer's choice) meaning "create Unix sockets, and remove or rename non-folder entries, beneath this path; nothing else". The Linux backend maps it to exactly the make-socket and remove-file Landlock rights (both in Landlock ABI 1). The macOS profile renderer refuses a rule carrying it (render error, no start: fail closed); Windows has no file rules (Q5). Never expressed as `AccessWrite`. Backend-lead runs GitNexus impact on `PathRule` and `accessToLandlockRights` and lists every reader of `PathRule.Access` in the gate brief; security-lead reviews (`pkg/sandbox` is a focus area) |
| D4 | Socket cleanup | LibreOffice removes its own socket when it exits normally (start 1 with exit 81, start 2 with exit 0; measured in variants B and C). No gateway-side socket removal is designed in this landing; a killed start's leftover socket is accepted for this landing (VC-1) |
| D5 | Disclosure (FR-062) | The user guide and the landing text state plainly, for Linux: LibreOffice may create its control socket and remove entries in the shared `/tmp`; it reads `/dev/urandom` and the account list; the stricter per-conversion folder and the converter network restriction are not switched on yet (#1856). *Corrected 2026-11-21 (§3.9 F1):* name only what #1856 still adds, the converter system-call filter that refuses LibreOffice's own IPv4 and IPv6 sockets and the per-conversion private folder, and say nothing about the network rules in force today (VC-4). Never a "fully locked down" or equivalent claim (chief's condition). §17 row "2026-11-16, §3.6" |

*What stays as before §3.5 for this landing.* No per-call socket folder on Linux; the §3.4 D2/D3 socket-folder rows and the D4 socket-folder pattern stay macOS-only; MV-2's Linux list stays the four entries.

*Corrections and reversals for this landing.* §3.5's in-place corrections 2 (Linux `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`), 3 (Linux socket folder in AD-7/AD-8), 4 (Linux socket-folder row in D8c), 6 (T-97 on Linux) and 7 (T-102 Linux write rules) describe D8e; for this landing each place now states the variant-C state, with the D8e text kept and marked "#1856". Corrections 1 (the renderer and extractor keep a port-0 rule, so TCP bind is not denied — now also true of the converter until #1856) and 5 (the reused-name recovery limit, which applies to the macOS socket folder too) stay. Correction 8 is replaced: W1's RED range adds T-114…T-118, and T-107…T-113 move to #1856. D8c's "Nothing else" sentence "No rule ever names `/tmp` itself" is false under variant C and is qualified in place.

*Risks.*

| Risk | Handling |
|---|---|
| **Accepted risk until #1856 (founder, 2026-11-16):** during a conversion the converter may create, remove and rename entries anywhere under the service user's `/tmp` (Landlock rules are hierarchical). In detail: it can create Unix sockets anywhere under `/tmp` where the service account may create entries, and delete or rename non-folder entries the service account may delete there (its own files and sockets, other programs' sockets of that account, CI scratch), and then put its own socket at a freed name, so that program's clients could talk to the converter. It cannot read, list or write file contents there, and cannot create regular files, folders or links | Accepted, disclosed (FR-062); closed by #1856, whose design (§3.5 D8e) grants nothing on `/tmp` itself |
| The converter can read the local account list (`/etc/passwd`: account names, home folders, shells; no password hashes; already readable by every local account) and could render it into a preview an agent reads | Consequence of founder Q1 A; disclosed (FR-062) |
| The `/etc/passwd` read may have no effect if the converter's start thread also carries the gateway's own boot sandbox, whose rules grant `/etc/nsswitch.conf` and `/dev/urandom` but not `/etc/passwd` (`pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go::DefaultPolicyForModel`, `readOnlySystem`); the socket would then be set to mode 0777 again, silently. Whether the boot domain applies is the open §3.4 D8c / security-review question | **Unknown** at the time. **Resolved 2026-11-19 (§3.7 BR-1.4):** the boot domain does apply, and BR-1 adds `/etc/passwd` to the confined boot set. T-114 asserts the per-user socket and fails, never skips, if the read has no effect (VC-3) |
| No network restriction beyond today's for the converter: TCP connect refused where Landlock supports it, TCP bind allowed on a port the system picks (port-0 rule), UDP not covered | Disclosed as "not switched on yet" (FR-062); closed for the converter by #1856. *2026-11-21, §3.9 F1:* the disclosure names only what #1856 adds (the converter filter and the private folder); the facts in this row stay internal (VC-4) |
| A start killed by Stop, the 120 s deadline, the memory limit, a crash or a gateway crash leaves its socket in `/tmp` (measured unconfined: SIGKILL leaves the socket; each call has its own socket name, so nothing reuses it) | Accepted for this landing (VC-1); tracked under epic #1855 and #1856 |
| A data folder or workspace that lies under `/tmp` falls inside the remove right | Documented limitation of this landing (VC-2): FR-051 narrowed, user guide states it (FR-062), tests keep their homes outside `/tmp`; tracked under #1856 |
| The rights were measured outside Omnipus | T-114 is the proving run inside Omnipus, under enforcing Landlock |

*Open points, ruled by team-lead 2026-11-16 (labels VC-1…VC-4, renamed from O1–O4 so they cannot be confused with the §1 measurement row O3).*

| # | Question | Why it matters | Ruling (team-lead, 2026-11-16) |
|---|---|---|---|
| VC-1 | Must a killed start leave no socket in this landing (gateway-side removal by computed name plus recovery, the 2026-11-10 design's cleanup), or is the leftover socket an accepted residue moved to #1855? | T-115 asserts "nothing left behind" only for calls that end without a kill; a leftover socket is still mode-protected under variant C but accumulates | **Accepted for this landing.** The leftover socket after a killed start is an edge case tracked under epic #1855 and #1856; T-115 keeps asserting only calls that end without a kill |
| VC-2 | FR-051 and T-92 require the owner record to be out of the converter's write reach. With the data folder under `/tmp` (Go test homes from `t.TempDir()` usually are; a CI or custom `OMNIPUS_HOME` may be), the remove right covers the record: the converter could delete it, though not forge it (no make-regular-file right). Move test homes outside `/tmp` and state the limit, or exclude the case otherwise? | T-92 on Linux may turn red under variant C for this reason, not for a code defect | **Test homes and data folders used by tests are pinned outside `/tmp`** (every Linux preview test that runs the real converter, T-92 and T-114…T-118 included; how they are placed is the developer's choice, but never `t.TempDir()` while it resolves under `/tmp`). A data folder under `/tmp` is a **documented limitation of this landing**: FR-051 is narrowed accordingly and the user guide states it (§17 row "2026-11-16, §3.6"); tracked under #1856 |
| VC-3 | Should T-114 also assert the founder's stated purpose (the socket name carries the account number and the socket is not world-writable) inside Omnipus? | Without it the 0777 exposure could return silently (risk row 3) | **Yes.** T-114 asserts that the converter's socket name carries the gateway account's numeric id and that the socket is not writable by other accounts. If `/etc/passwd` is not readable under the gateway's sandbox, T-114 fails and shows that; it never skips |
| VC-4 | `docs/documents.md` says the preview programs on Linux "cannot open or accept TCP connections where the kernel supports that rule". With the port-0 rule this is false for all three programs. FR-062 requires correcting it for LibreOffice; correcting it for the renderer and extractor would describe T3, which founder flag 3 A keeps out of the public guide until fixed. Narrow the sentence to LibreOffice and say nothing about the other two, or ask the founder? | The page would otherwise keep a false network claim, against the chief's condition | **Delete** the Linux sentence ("on Linux they cannot open or accept TCP connections where the kernel supports that rule (UDP is not blocked)"); do not narrow it to LibreOffice. The page makes no claim about network protection for any of the three programs on Linux in this landing. A required user-doc correction in §17 (implementing lead drafts, docs-verifier audits) |

*Alternatives considered.* **§3.5 D8e now:** deferred by the founder's split (#1856). **Variant B** (no account-list reads): the socket is mode 0777 for each conversion, so any local account can connect to the control channel. **Make-socket only, with the gateway removing the socket by computed name** (the 2026-11-10 recommendation): no remove right on `/tmp`, but more code and a socket-name computation; the founder chose variant C. **`AccessWrite` on `/tmp`:** also grants creating, writing and truncating files and folders; contradicts FR-061.

*Consequences.* Positive: Office preview can work on Linux with measured rights and a small change; the founder's end-to-end landing is not blocked on #1856. Negative: the accepted `/tmp` risk, the readable account list and the unchanged network rules until #1856; one new `pkg/sandbox` access value (security-lead review); open points VC-1…VC-4, ruled by team-lead 2026-11-16 (above). Accepted by team-lead 2026-11-16: T-118, the FR-062 numbering and the new narrow `pkg/sandbox` access value. Neutral: no contract change; macOS and Windows unchanged; user pages change (§17, row "2026-11-16, §3.6").

*Affected components.* `pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go` (new access value), `pkg/sandbox/sandbox_linux.go::LinuxBackend.accessToLandlockRights`, `pkg/sandbox/seatbelt_profile.go` (refuse the value); `pkg/preview/confine.go::confinement` (Linux-only socket-and-remove folder list; name: developer's choice), `pkg/preview/confine_unix.go::confinement.policy`, `pkg/preview/programs.go::officeConversion.run` (Linux branch: the `/tmp` entry and the three reads); `scripts/office-preview-real/real-tests.txt` (T-114…T-116); `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`, `docs/documents.md` (§17). Tests T-114…T-118. **security-lead must review the new `pkg/sandbox` access value and its Linux and macOS handling before this landing lands** (team-lead ruling, 2026-11-16).

### 3.7 Dated amendment 2026-11-19 — PDF fallback to text (G12), installer packaging (PK), confined Linux boot reads (BR-1, was "Q1 B") (architect; cloud chief ruling G12, founder packaging decisions, architect gate finding F3)

Status: decided. G12: cloud chief ruling, 2026-11-19, relayed to the founder. PK: founder, 2026-11-19 (Mac terminal, question tool). BR-1: founder decision of 2026-11-18, already implemented (commit `c2be326a0`), recorded here because the spec lacked it (architect gate finding F3). Adds FR-063…FR-065, B-79…B-81, T-119…T-121 and U-M12. Amends, in place and marked "2026-11-19, §3.7": §1, AD-4, AD-6 (assembled request media row), AD-12, §3.6 D2 and its risk row 3, §4.1 (row 2 upload cell; the recovery sentence; the SF-08 note after the table), US-2, §10, FR-017, FR-019, FR-024, S-1, T-33, T-70, §15, §16 (U-L2, U-L3, the closing note), §17, §18 (W1, W2), §19, §21 and §22. Baseline: `landing/office-preview` at `376c40e2d`. Sources, all outside the repository: the full-feature gate reports for #1713 in the office-q1b gate scratch area (code-reviewer W1, silent-failure-hunter SF-08/SF-09, pr-test-analyzer F2, architect F2/F3); the founder's packaging decision record (`decisions.md`, office-pack scratch area) and the packaging plan it points to (`packaging-sizing.md`, items P1–P9).

#### G12 — oversized and rejected PDFs fall back to extracted text

*Context.* §4.1 required, for an upload to a model that reads PDFs natively and also sees images, page images plus text plus a paging note when the PDF is larger than the native limit. The code sends extracted text instead: `pkg/agent/loop_media.go::resolveMediaRefsWithOffload` asks `encodePDFToDataURL` for the native form with the limit from `pkg/agent/document_preview_media.go::nativePDFLimits`; when the size is refused it calls `buildDocumentInjection` and never reaches `uploadPDFPageImages`, which runs only for models without the PDF modality. The user guide documents the text behaviour (`docs/documents.md`, "PDFs you attach to a message"). The gate flagged the disagreement (code-reviewer W1, silent-failure-hunter SF-09) and a missing proof that a provider's rejection of a PDF attached by a tool is recovered (pr-test-analyzer F2).

*Decision.*

| # | Rule | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| G12.1 | Oversized upload | An uploaded PDF larger than the native limit (the agent media size limit, tightened by the catalog's `pdf_limits`), sent to a model that reads PDFs natively, gets the **extracted text**, not page images, whether or not the model also sees images (§4.1 row 2, upload cell). Unchanged: `preview_document` and `read_file` over the native limit still deliver page images with the size note (`pkg/agent/document_preview_media.go::attachDocumentPreview`), and an upload to a model without the PDF modality keeps pages 1–10 as images, the text and the paging note (§4.1 row 3) |
| G12.3 | `read_file` on a PDF without extractable text (SF-08; cloud chief ruling 2026-11-19) | `read_file` on a PDF with no extractable text (for example a scan) does not offer the native or image form; the file is read as any other file. This matches the code and `docs/documents.md`. The native/image form for such PDFs is deferred to #1918; re-opened at the 2026-11-22 12:00Z checkpoint only if the fix round has spare capacity |
| G12.2 | Provider rejection (FR-063) | When the provider rejects a native PDF with a classified PDF-input rejection, the turn retries with that document's **extracted text** in place of the PDF, for every entry point (upload, `read_file`, `preview_document`), and the turn completes. The recovery request carries the text itself when extraction succeeds, and the existing honest notice when it does not. The rejected PDF is not sent again in that turn, neither by the recovery request nor by a later request of the same turn. Page images are not a recovery form. The narrow classification is unchanged: authentication, unknown-model, size, schema and policy errors are not turned into text retries (`pkg/agent/media_downgrade.go::TryMediaDowngrade`, T-51) |

*Code at `376c40e2d` (read, not run).* G12.1 is what the code does (above). G12.2 holds for **uploads**: `pkg/agent/loop_media.go::downgradePDFMediaToText` replaces the PDF in the call's messages, and the retry is built from them. G12.2 does **not** hold for **`read_file` and `preview_document`**: their native PDF is attached request-locally inside `pkg/agent/loop_run_turn_attempt.go::agentLoopRunTurn.runProviderAttempt` (through `attachTurnDocumentPreviews`), after the call's messages were recorded by the `callLLM` closure in `pkg/agent/loop_run_turn_response.go::agentLoopRunTurnResponse.callLLMWithRetries`; `TryMediaDowngrade` and `pkg/agent/loop_run_turn.go::agentLoopRunTurnFallbacks.tryPDFTextFallback` therefore find no PDF to convert, and `pkg/agent/document_preview_media.go::agentLoopRunTurn.previewForms` does not consult the turn's PDF-downgrade guard, so a retry or a later request attaches the same PDF again. The consequence (the turn fails instead of completing with text) is **Inferred**, high confidence, as in pr-test-analyzer F2. This is implementation work for backend-lead; T-120 is its acceptance test.

*Alternatives considered.* **Page images for oversized uploads** (SF-09's recommendation): rejected by the ruling; more code in the upload resolver, and code and user guide already agree on text. **Page images as the rejection-recovery form:** rejected by the ruling; text works for every model.

*Consequences.* Positive: spec, code and user guide agree for uploads; one recovery form for all entry points. Negative: an image-capable native model gets no page images of an oversized upload, so layout is lost, and a scanned PDF yields only the extraction-failure notice (code-reviewer W1); that upload branch makes no workspace copy and names no path (`resolveMediaRefsWithOffload`, read), so the paging note of §4.1 row 3 does not apply to it. Neutral: no contract change; the upload text of `docs/documents.md` is already correct; one sentence on rejection recovery is added (§17 row "2026-11-19, §3.7").

*SF-08 deferred (cloud chief ruling, 2026-11-19, G12.3).* silent-failure-hunter SF-08 is a different branch from G12.1/G12.2. The spec now states what the code and the user guide already do: `read_file` on a PDF with no extractable text (for example a scan) does not offer the native or image form; `pkg/tools/read_file_pdf.go::ReadFileTool.pdfDocumentResult` returns nil when text extraction fails, and the file is read the way `read_file` reads any other file (raw text or the binary-file refusal), as `docs/documents.md` states. Offering the native or image form for such PDFs is deferred to #1918; re-opened at the 2026-11-22 12:00Z checkpoint only if the fix round has spare capacity. `preview_document` is not affected (§4.3 "Text empty (scanned PDF)").

*Affected components.* `pkg/agent/loop_run_turn_attempt.go`, `loop_run_turn_response.go`, `loop_run_turn.go`, `media_downgrade.go`, `document_preview_media.go`, `loop_media.go` (G12.2 for the tool entry points); `docs/documents.md` (§17). Tests T-51, T-70, T-120.

#### G5 — what the 32 MiB request media budget covers (cloud chief ruling and team-lead approval, 2026-11-19)

*Context.* The gate fix for G5 (silent-failure-hunter SF-06; commit `71e17ce88` on `local/office-gatefix`) made uploads and tool previews share AD-6's 32 MiB per-request media budget, as required. It reads AD-6 literally: `pkg/agent/document_preview_media.go::attachTurnDocumentPreviews` subtracts `requestDataMedia`, the decoded size of every inline image or PDF already in the request, including screenshots from earlier turns. In a chat with more than 32 MiB of earlier screenshots every new preview then reaches the model only as "not re-sent; call preview_document again", calling again meets the same limit, and older media wins over newer previews, against AD-6's "newest calls first". AD-6's budget was meant to bound the media this feature adds to one request, not the whole chat's history (architect, read-only ruling; option A with two additions, approved by team-lead in line with the cloud chief's ruling).

*Decision.*

| # | Rule |
|---|---|
| G5.1 | **Scope.** The 32 MiB covers this turn's document media only: native PDFs and upload page images from uploads in this turn's own user message, and `preview_document` / `read_file` previews (the turn's preview store). Ordinary images (screenshots, inspection images) and all earlier-turn media are outside it and keep their existing rules (`fitOrdinaryImageMediaToBudget`; the upload allowance of 20 pages and 32 MiB) |
| G5.2 | **Turn boundary.** "This turn" is the request's messages from the turn's own user message onward. If no reliable boundary exists in the turn state, the developer stops and asks; never infer it by content matching |
| G5.3 | **Order.** This turn's uploads count first (they come with the person's message and cannot be called again); previews share the rest, newest call first |
| G5.4 | **Document count unchanged.** The provider's per-request native-PDF count is an external hard limit: every PDF in the request, earlier turns included, still counts against it; a preview past it goes as page images, as today |
| G5.5 | **No futile retry.** If the newest preview gets no media from the 32 MiB, the model receives its extracted text with a note giving the reason (this request's 32 MiB is used by this message's attachments), never `[pages not re-sent; call preview_document again]`. Older previews squeezed out by newer ones keep that marker |
| G5.6 | **Count used up, no page images** (*2026-11-21, §3.9; architect gate A5; code `ef706c8b2`, `56e5313d3`*). When a preview's native PDF is left out because the provider's per-request PDF count is used up (G5.4) and no page images go for it (the model sees no images, page images are unavailable, or none fit), the model receives the preview's extracted text with the note `[model received: extracted text; the provider's per-request PDF count limit (<n>) is used up by other PDFs in this request]`; when page images were unavailable, `; page images unavailable: <reason>` comes before the count reason, and a missing budget (G5.5) adds its own reason after it. The note never says the model reads text only. A preview with no stored text gets the text extracted from its own PDF bytes, as in G12.2 (`pkg/agent/loop_media.go::pdfFallbackText`, with the existing notice when extraction fails). For `read_file` (`ModelOnly`), whose retained result already is the text, no count note is added (`pkg/agent/document_preview_media.go::attachDocumentPreview`). Test T-133 |

*Tests.* `TestOfficePreview_UploadAndToolShareRequestBudget` (or a sibling) adds: more than 32 MiB of earlier-turn screenshots, then a new preview → the preview reaches the model in full; this turn's uploads filling the 32 MiB → the newest preview arrives as text with the reason note. Not a pass if the screenshots are placed in the current turn's messages. Traces FR-031 and T-59 (AD-6 budgets).

*Alternatives considered.* **Literal reading (B):** blocks every preview in a long chat with screenshots, and no action inside the chat can fix it. **A second, separate budget:** brings back the double counting G5 fixed.

*Consequences.* Positive: previews work in long chats; uploads and previews still share one bound. Negative: a whole request can carry more than 32 MiB of media in total (earlier-turn and ordinary images on top), as it could before this feature. Neutral: no contract change; the user guide's per-request limit sentence (commit `71e17ce88`, `docs/documents.md`) is checked by docs-verifier against this scope.

#### PK — installer packaging (founder, 2026-11-19)

*Context.* §10 described delivery per install route, but the gate commit built none of it (architect gate finding F2): no component release files, no installer options, Docker on Alpine with the binary only, and the Intel Mac on operator registration. Two facts from the packaging plan: no installable release has ever been published, and the bundled Chrome never reached the release archives (#1913).

*Decision.*

| # | Rule | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| PK1 | Scope | Every documented install route: the one-line installer (Linux amd64 and arm64, macOS arm64 and amd64), deb/rpm, Docker, and Windows registration |
| PK2 | Intel Mac | darwin/amd64 is supported, built and released: its own main archive and both components, installed by the one-line installer exactly as on Apple silicon. Operator registration stays available on every platform but is no longer the Intel Mac route |
| PK3 | Docker | Base image Debian slim (glibc) instead of Alpine; a larger image is accepted. The image carries the official LibreOffice 26.8.x Linux build and the glibc renderer, each packaged with its manifest, at `/usr/local/share/omnipus/office` and `/usr/local/share/omnipus/pdfium` (the resolver's `<exe>/../share/omnipus/<slot>` for `/usr/local/bin/omnipus`, `pkg/preview/component/component.go::Resolver.PackagedCandidates`). No distribution package (registration accepts only 26.8.x, `pkg/preview/component/register.go::SupportedOfficeSeries`), no registration at image build, no musl build |
| PK4 | Delivery | The components are **separate release downloads**, `office_<os>_<arch>.tar.gz` and `pdfium_<os>_<arch>.tar.gz`, each listed in `checksums.txt`; the main archives carry no component. The installer fetches both by default, checks each against `checksums.txt` (a mismatch refuses the install) and unpacks them where the resolver looks (§10). `--without-office` and `--without-pdf-renderer` skip one; a re-run with an opt-out removes that component if an earlier run installed it. `OMNIPUS_INSTALL_BASE_URL` points every download (archive, `checksums.txt`, components) at another release folder, with checksums still verified (testing a release before it is published) |
| PK5 | Windows | Release file `pdfium_windows_amd64.zip` holds the renderer folder for `omnipus pdf-renderer register <folder>`. LibreOffice on Windows stays operator-installed and registered with `omnipus office register`. No Windows Omnipus binary is released (#113) |
| PK6 | deb/rpm | Package `omnipus` carries both components at `/usr/share/omnipus/office` and `/usr/share/omnipus/pdfium`, with LibreOffice's library dependencies. The `omnipus-nooffice` variant is **deferred to #1912**; until it ships, deb/rpm always carry Office (U-L2 deferred with it) |
| PK7 | Release contents check (FR-064) | A check lists what the release files really contain and fails, naming each missing item, on missing components (a component download, its `checksums.txt` entry, its manifest or programs; a deb/rpm or Docker image without both components) and on missing bundled Chrome (a main archive of a Chrome-for-Testing platform without it; #1913). It runs in the release pipelines and the packaging dry run (T-119) |
| PK8 | Fallback | B0 of the packaging plan (renderer zips plus registration-only texts) only if the release dry run is not green by 2026-11-22 12:00Z, and only after asking the founder |

*Code.* At `376c40e2d` none of PK exists (architect F2, re-checked: no `--without-office` in `scripts/install.sh`, Alpine in `docker/Dockerfile.goreleaser`). The pushed branch `packaging/office-dryrun` at `f2b47e9e1`, not part of this baseline, carries an implementation: `.goreleaser.yaml` (darwin/amd64 build; the nine component files in `checksum.extra_files` and `release.extra_files`; deb/rpm contents), `docker/Dockerfile.goreleaser` (`debian:trixie-slim`, both components under `/usr/local/share/omnipus`), `scripts/install.sh` (both options, `OMNIPUS_INSTALL_BASE_URL`, Intel Mac), `scripts/office-pack/build-components.sh`, `scripts/release/check-release-contents.sh`. Read for the names above only; its acceptance is the dry run, T-119 and the §16 rows, not this amendment.

*Alternatives considered* (packaging plan §5). **B1** (installer only, no Docker): leaves Windows and Docker users without the feature. **B2** (an `omnipus office install` download command): a new outbound network path and more new code in a security focus area. **B-full** (with `omnipus-nooffice`): does not fit the date; deferred to #1912. **Components inside the main archive:** every install pays the size, and an opt-out cannot shrink the download.

*Consequences.* Positive: the "re-run the Omnipus installer" texts (`pkg/preview/service.go`, `src/components/chat/PreviewUnavailableCard.tsx`, `docs/documents.md`) become true on the installer routes; Docker users get both components without registration; the Intel Mac gets the installer route. Negative: the default install grows by about 210 MB on Linux and about 300 MB on macOS, and the Docker image several-fold (estimates from the packaging plan, **not measured**); deb/rpm users cannot leave Office out until #1912; the release path is unproven until the dry run is green (#1913). Neutral: Hard Constraint #1 holds; the components are separate programs found through manifests (ADR-090, native-component exception); no contract change.

*Affected components.* `.goreleaser.yaml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `nightly.yml`, the component-build and packaging-dry-run workflows, `docker/Dockerfile.goreleaser`, `scripts/install.sh`, `scripts/office-pack/`, `scripts/release/check-release-contents.sh`, the packaged-manifest writer (`internal/tools/office-manifest`, `pkg/preview/component`); user pages (§17). Tests T-28, T-33, T-119; rows U-M6, U-M9, U-M12, U-L1, U-L3, U-W1.

#### BR-1 — the confined Linux boot set gains three reads (founder decision of 2026-11-18, called "Q1 B" in code)

*Label.* Code comments, commits and test names call this decision "Q1 B" (`pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go::confinedBootExtraReads`, commit `c2be326a0`). That label collides with §3.1's unrelated "Q1 B" alternative of 2026-10-31 and sits beside §1's "Q1 (2026-11-16) A", so this spec calls it **BR-1**. The code keeps its own wording.

*Context.* §3.6 D2 sets the converter's Linux rights only in `pkg/preview/programs.go::officeConversion.run`, and §3.6 risk row 3 left **Unknown** whether the gateway's own boot sandbox, which lacks `/etc/passwd`, also binds the converter. It does: Landlock domains stack, so a converter started from a thread that carries the gateway's boot layer is refused anything the boot set lacks, whatever its own rules grant. A trace of 600 real LibreOffice conversions (kernel 6.12, Landlock ABI 6) showed the converter needs `/etc/passwd` (without it the socket name loses the account number and the socket is created mode 0777) and the system font folders (without them fonts vanish: CJK lines render blank, named fonts are substituted). Cited from the code comment on `confinedBootExtraReads` and commit `c2be326a0`; the trace itself was not re-read by the architect (**Inferred** for the measurement, **Verified** for the code).

*Decision.*

| # | Rule | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| BR-1.1 | Three reads (FR-065) | On Linux under `sandbox.filesystem_model: confined`, the gateway's boot set (`pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go::DefaultPolicyForModel`) adds exactly: `/etc/passwd` (read; the Linux backend reduces it to read-file), `/usr/share/fonts` and `/usr/local/share/fonts` (read, covering the folder tree; skipped when absent). Read only, never execute. Measured not needed and left out: `/etc/fonts`, `/etc/ld.so.cache`. *Amended 2026-11-20 (§3.8 BR-2, founder Q100 A):* "exactly" now applies to these three **reads** only; the confined Linux startup rules also carry BR-2's read-and-execute entries for the installed preview programs and the omnipus program file, built by `sandbox.DefaultBootPolicyForModel` |
| BR-1.2 | Boot set only | Per-turn and tool policies do not get them: `pkg/sandbox/derive_from_fspolicy.go::DeriveKernelPolicy` starts from `basePolicyForModel`, which excludes them. The open model, macOS and Windows are unchanged. *2026-11-20, §3.8:* the same holds for the BR-2 entries, which only the gateway startup's `sandbox.DefaultBootPolicyForModel` adds |
| BR-1.3 | Converter rules unchanged | §3.6 D2 and FR-061 stay as written for the converter's own rules; the BR-1 reads are a separate, gateway-wide layer, not converter rights. Removing them to "restore conformance" with §3.6 D2 is a regression: on confined Linux it silently brings back the mode-0777 socket and the lost fonts |
| BR-1.4 | Risk row 3 resolved | The boot layer does bind the converter; BR-1 supplies `/etc/passwd`. T-114's boot case (`pkg/preview/functional_boot_linux_test.go::TestPreviewOffice_T114_ConfinedGatewayBootAccountLookup`) asserts the per-user socket under the confined boot, and T-121 pins the three reads |

*Alternatives considered.* **Grant the reads only in the converter's own rules:** does not work; the stacked boot domain still refuses them. **Leave them out:** the socket is open to every local account during a conversion and fonts are lost; not chosen.

*Consequences.* Positive: Office preview on confined Linux keeps the per-user socket and the installed fonts. Negative: the three reads apply to every child process that carries only the gateway's boot sandbox, not just the converter; which children start without a per-turn policy is not listed (**Inferred**; noted for the #1897 security list, architect F3). Neutral: no contract change; the user guide already describes it (`docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`, commit `c2be326a0`), and docs-verifier audits that text (§17).

*Affected components.* `pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go` (`DefaultPolicyForModel`, `confinedBootExtraReads`, `basePolicyForModel`), `pkg/sandbox/derive_from_fspolicy.go::DeriveKernelPolicy`, `.github/workflows/pr.yml` (fonts installed for the `office-preview-real` job), `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`. `pkg/sandbox` is a security-lead focus area. Tests T-114, T-118, T-121.

### 3.8 Dated amendment 2026-11-20 — confined Linux startup rules grant the installed components (BR-2; founder Q100 A) (architect)

Status: decided.
