Founder Q100 = A (2026-11-20 04:35Z, relayed by the cloud chief and team-lead): on Linux under the confined file model, the gateway's startup rules let the installed preview programs be read and run, plus the omnipus program file, and nothing more; never for agents; the "exactly three reads" text is amended. Adds BR-2, FR-066, B-82…B-88, T-122…T-129 and U-L6. Amends, in place and marked "2026-11-20, §3.8": the header, §1 (new Q100 row; BR-1 row), §3.7 BR-1 (BR-1.1, BR-1.2), §9 MV-1, §12 B-81, §13 T-121, §14 FR-065, §15, §16, §17, §18 (W1, W2), §21 and §22. Baseline: `landing/office-preview` at `714e9b99f`. **Review:** the founder requires a security-lead review and an architect re-pass by a different architect instance; the author of BR-1 and of this section does not re-pass it.

*Context.* "Startup rules" here means the gateway's boot set, `pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go::DefaultPolicyForModel`, built once in `pkg/gateway/sandbox_apply.go` (`as.policy = sandbox.DefaultPolicyForModel(...)`). Under the confined model on Linux it grants read and execute only on the fixed list in `::basePolicyForModel` (`readOnlySystem`: `/lib`, `/lib64`, `/usr/lib`, `/usr/lib64`, `/usr/bin`, `/opt` and a few `/etc`, `/proc`, `/sys`, `/dev` entries), plus the BR-1 reads (`::confinedBootExtraReads`) and the operator's `sandbox.allowed_exec_paths`, which ships empty on Linux (`pkg/config/exec_paths.go::DefaultAllowedExecPaths`). There is nothing for `/usr/share`, `/usr/local/share` or `/usr/local/bin`. The packaged slots are found relative to the program file (`pkg/preview/component/component.go::Resolver.PackagedCandidates`: `<exe>/../office` and `<exe>/../share/omnipus/<slot>`), so deb/rpm use `/usr/share/omnipus/office` and `/usr/share/omnipus/pdfium` with `/usr/bin/omnipus`, and the installer and Docker use `/usr/local/share/omnipus/…` with `/usr/local/bin/omnipus` (§10; §3.7 PK3, PK6). Landlock restricts only the thread that applies it (`pkg/sandbox/sandbox_linux.go::LinuxBackend.ApplyWithMode` calls `landlock_restrict_self` with flags 0); threads created from that thread inherit the restriction, and a launcher started from such a thread is limited by both layers (§3.7 BR-1 Context; `pkg/sandbox/hardened_exec_held.go::StartLockedWithPolicyHeld`). The gateway reads and hashes the component on every call (`pkg/preview/service.go::Service.rendererComponent`, `::officeComponent` → `Resolver.Resolve`, `Component.Verify`), and every preview program starts through the run-limited launcher, which is the omnipus program file itself (`pkg/preview/launcher/launcher.go::Command` runs `Spec.Self`; `pkg/preview/shared.go::StartGateway` sets `Self: resolver.Executable`).

So on confined Linux, on every shipped layout, a call that runs on a thread carrying the startup rules cannot read the component ("packaged manifest unreadable: permission denied", reported as damaged) or cannot start the launcher. On the installer and Docker layouts the launcher's own file is outside the startup rules, so the PDF renderer and the text fallback fail too. A custom prefix under `/opt` already works. **Inferred** (reasoned from the code, not run): the failure is intermittent in a freshly started process and constant after an in-app restart that ran on a restricted thread, because a Landlock domain survives `execve` (`pkg/gateway/rest_gateway_restart.go::reExecProcess`); on the installer layout such a restart cannot even re-execute `/usr/local/bin/omnipus` today. The shipped default is the open model (`pkg/config/defaults.go`, `FilesystemModel: open`), so default installs are not affected; the ADR **Reads and execute default open; writes stay confined** (ADR-062) requires multi-tenant installs to pin `confined`. No existing test covers confined plus packaged components: T-114's boot case and the Q1 B real-program test grant the binary's folder or the LibreOffice folder themselves.

*Decision.*

| # | Rule | Detail |
|---|---|---|
| BR-2.1 | What is granted (FR-065) | On Linux under `sandbox.filesystem_model: confined`, the startup rules add **read and execute** (exactly `AccessRead \| AccessExecute`) on: **(a)** the real path (symlinks resolved) of each packaged slot of LibreOffice and of the PDF renderer, in `Resolver.PackagedCandidates` order, that exists when the startup rules are built, (the first Linux slot, `<exe>/../office`, for example `/usr/office` for `/usr/bin/omnipus`, normally does not exist and is skipped as missing), whether or not it holds a valid manifest (a slot is trusted by its location, §3.2 item 1; its content is still verified on every call as before); **(b)** the real path of the `root` named in each registered manifest (`component.RegisteredDir(home, kind)`, file `manifest.json`) that passes `component.ParseManifest` and exists; **(c)** the resolved omnipus program **file** (the path `component.NewResolver` resolves, which is the file `launcher.Command` runs and `reExecProcess` re-executes), as a file rule, never its folder. Order: LibreOffice (registered root, then packaged slots), the PDF renderer (the same), then the program file; a path listed twice is granted once *2026-11-21, §3.9 OF-4:* on Linux (b) lists only a root strictly inside Omnipus's own folder; any other registered root is not listed and is noted as `<kind> registered root <root> skipped: outside Omnipus's own folder <own>` |
| BR-2.2 | Where it is built (team-lead ruling within Q100 A, 2026-11-20) | A new sibling function used **only** by the gateway startup: `sandbox.DefaultBootPolicyForModel(model, homePath, allowedPaths, allowedExecPaths, warnFn, bindPorts, startupGrants)`. `sandbox.DefaultPolicyForModel` keeps its current signature and delegates to it with `startupGrants` = nil, so every caller other than the gateway startup structurally cannot receive the grants; `basePolicyForModel` is unchanged and never sees them, so `pkg/sandbox/derive_from_fspolicy.go::DeriveKernelPolicy` and the tool policies cannot carry them. The gateway computes the list **once**, before it applies the startup rules (in `pkg/gateway/sandbox_apply.go`, replacing its `sandbox.DefaultPolicyForModel(...)` call with `sandbox.DefaultBootPolicyForModel(...)`), using a new helper in `pkg/preview/component`; backend-lead names the helper. The list is never written into `sandbox.allowed_exec_paths`: that list is the operator's, an operator's own list replaces the seed, and it reaches every per-turn policy. One Info log line at start, `sandbox.component_grants`, names each granted path and each refused or skipped entry with its reason. The gateway keeps the granted list for BR-2.5. Because the list is computed once and Landlock rules can only narrow, never widen, a component registered, moved or re-installed while Omnipus runs is not granted until Omnipus is stopped and started (BR-2.5, BR-2.7); the in-app restart keeps the old startup rules (see *Consequences*) |
| BR-2.3 | What is excluded | No write access on any entry. No parent folder of a slot (`<prefix>/share/omnipus`, `<prefix>/share`, `<prefix>`) and no `bin` folder (`<prefix>/bin`; for example not `/usr/local/bin` as a whole). A slot or registered root that does not exist is skipped. Refused, with a warning, and not granted, by the same rules as `pkg/sandbox/exec_paths.go::buildExecPathRules`: `/`; a restricted system path (`::isSystemRestricted`); and any entry that lies inside **or contains** a folder the startup rules make writable (the data folder `$OMNIPUS_HOME`, `/tmp`, `$TMPDIR`, each writable `allowed_paths` entry), judged on both the declared and the symlink-resolved path (`::overlapsAny`). The open model, macOS and Windows are unchanged: the input is ignored there and the result is byte-identical to the result without it |
| BR-2.4 | Never for agents (*rewritten 2026-11-21, §3.9 OF-6; the 2026-11-20 text promised more than the sandbox gives, architect gate A2, docs-verifier F2*) | The startup entries never reach agents: per-turn policies (`DeriveKernelPolicy`, built from `basePolicyForModel`) and tool policies carry none of the BR-2.1 entries and no path under them. On confined Linux every preview program (the files of a packaged or registered LibreOffice or PDF renderer) lies in a packaged slot or, since §3.9 OF-3 and OF-4, strictly inside Omnipus's own folder `dir(E)/../share/omnipus`; no rule of an agent's sandbox covers those folders by default, so an agent's shell child cannot read or run a preview program, even on a thread that also carries the startup rules (the layers stack). **Two exceptions, stated exactly:** (i) the omnipus program file is installed so that a packaged slot or Omnipus's own folder lies inside a folder every agent command may read and run (`readOnlySystem` in `basePolicyForModel`: `/lib`, `/lib64`, `/usr/lib`, `/usr/lib64`, `/usr/bin`, `/opt`), for example an installer run with `OMNIPUS_INSTALL_DIR=/opt/omnipus/bin`; agents can then read and run the programs through that folder; (ii) an operator's `sandbox.allowed_exec_paths` entry, or a read or execute access approved for a command (`fspolicy.PathGrant`, ADR-092 D7), that covers the folder gives that access to the commands it applies to. A `sandbox.allowed_paths` entry, a mount or any other write access covering a program folder is not a third exception: it already disables the component for everyone (§3.2, AD-4). No shipped route (deb/rpm, the installer's default and per-user folders, Docker) is exception (i). The omnipus program file (BR-2.1 (c)) is not a preview program: on the deb/rpm layout `/usr/bin/omnipus` lies under `/usr/bin`, which agent commands could read and run before BR-2; on the installer and Docker layouts `/usr/local/bin/omnipus` is outside their sandbox. Founder OF-Q1, 2026-11-21, founder, terminal question tool, relayed by team-lead: "Document only (Recommended)": (i) and (ii) are documented in the user guide (§17) and nothing beyond OF-3 and OF-4 is built |
| BR-2.5 | Installed, registered or replaced after the start (FR-066) | On Linux under the confined model with the kernel sandbox enforcing (`sandbox.CurrentLinuxBackend().EnforcingChildren()`), before reading any file of a component, the gateway checks that the folder it is about to use (the registered root, or the packaged slot) was granted at this start: the same real path, and the same device and inode numbers recorded when the rule was made (Landlock binds a rule to that folder, not to its name). If not, the component is not used: nothing in it is read and nothing is started. LibreOffice: the tool result is the MV-1 message `Office preview was installed or registered after Omnipus started. Stop and start Omnipus (for example with your service manager) to use it; the in-app restart is not enough in the confined sandbox.` PDF renderer: the existing reason code `renderer_damaged` (no new contract value, as for the unsafe-location case in `Service.rendererComponent`) with the reason text `PDF renderer was installed or registered after Omnipus started; stop and start Omnipus (for example with your service manager) to use it; the in-app restart is not enough in the confined sandbox`; the AD-15 fallbacks apply unchanged. An entry refused at start (BR-2.3) is reported with the existing texts: inside or containing a writable folder → `<component> is in a folder an agent can write (<folder>); disabled until that access is removed.`; `/` or a restricted system path → `Office preview is damaged (program folder refused by the sandbox: <path>). Reinstall or re-register the Office preview component.` (the renderer: `renderer_damaged`, the same reason). `doctor` is unchanged: it runs in its own process and cannot see the running gateway's startup rules (`pkg/app/internal/doctor/preview.go` builds its own resolver) *2026-11-21, §3.9:* on Linux a registered root outside Omnipus's own folder is refused by the resolver before this check, with the OF-4 texts (`doctor` reports it too, since the check sits in the resolver); a refused read while the record is active gives the RS-1 texts |
| BR-2.6 | Withdrawn (team-lead veto, 2026-11-20, simplest design first) | No rule. The in-app restart is not changed; its known limitation is recorded under *Consequences*. The number is kept so BR-2.7 and the rows that cite it stay stable |
| BR-2.7 | Restart hint from the registration commands (team-lead ruling within Q100 A, 2026-11-20) | After a **successful** `omnipus office register <folder>` or `omnipus pdf-renderer register <folder>` on Linux, when the configuration's `sandbox.filesystem_model` is `confined`, the command prints one extra line to standard output, after its existing `Registered <kind> <version> from <root>` line: `Stop and start Omnipus (for example with your service manager) to use this component; the in-app restart is not enough in the confined sandbox, which lets the running gateway use only components installed before it started.` The exit code is unchanged (0). If the configuration cannot be read, registration is refused loudly before any hint, because `pkg/app/internal/components/command.go::configuredWriteRoots` then reports a write root that covers everything and the AD-4 writer exclusion fails closed (AD-4 unchanged); no restart line is printed. Nothing extra is printed under the open model, on macOS or on Windows, or after a failed registration. The command does not restart anything itself |

*Alternatives considered.* **Pre-fill `sandbox.allowed_exec_paths` on Linux:** rejected; that list reaches every per-turn policy through `basePolicyForModel`, an operator's list replaces the seed, Linux is kept empty on purpose (`pkg/config/exec_paths.go`), and it cannot name registered folders. **Start the converter from a thread without the startup rules:** rejected; a Landlock domain cannot be removed and Go offers no guaranteed unrestricted thread for every call. **Declare confined plus Office unsupported, with a disclosure:** rejected by the founder (Q100); it breaks B-81 and FR-055 and leaves multi-tenant installs without the feature. **Grant `/usr/share` and `/usr/local/share` as a whole:** rejected; far too broad. **Grant the program file's folder instead of the file:** rejected; on the installer layout that is `/usr/local/bin` with every other program in it. **Make the in-app restart re-execute on a thread locked before the startup rules were applied (proposed as BR-2.6):** vetoed by team-lead, 2026-11-20 — simplest design first; it adds complexity and security surface for a rare case (a confined install that adds a component while running), and Go gives no guaranteed unrestricted thread.

*Risks and security-lead review points* (`pkg/sandbox` and gateway startup are security-lead focus areas).

| # | Point |
|---|---|
| 1 | The entries reach every child that starts with the startup rules only (no per-turn policy), not just the preview programs: the same class as BR-1's negative consequence; add it to the #1897 security list |
| 2 | The access bits are fixed in code (read and execute, never write), as `buildExecPathRules` fixes them |
| 3 | Refusals: `/`, restricted system paths, and inside-or-containing a writable folder, on declared and resolved paths |
| 4 | The program is granted as a file, not its folder |
| 5 | Trust in an operator-registered `root`: whether `RegisterOffice` / `RegisterRenderer` refuse a broad folder (for example a whole home folder) is **Unknown**; the containing-a-writable-folder refusal covers a root that contains the data folder or `/tmp`, not every broad root *2026-11-21, §3.9 OF-3:* on Linux a registered root lies strictly inside Omnipus's own folder, so a whole home folder, `/opt` or `/usr` cannot be registered there; on macOS and Windows registration still sets no breadth limit (security-lead, 2026-11-21) |
| 6 | Interaction with §3.2 (agents can never write to a program folder): unchanged and still required; BR-2 adds no write path |
| 7 | The in-app restart (`pkg/gateway/rest_gateway_restart.go::reExecProcess`) re-executes on whatever thread handles it, so the new process may inherit the old startup rules (known limitation, *Consequences*); the BR-2.1 (c) program-file grant lets that re-execution start at all on the installer layout. Security-lead confirms nothing else depends on the restart starting clean |
| 8 | A folder replaced in place after the start (for example a re-install while Omnipus runs) loses access until Omnipus is stopped and started; BR-2.5's inode check reports it instead of "damaged" |

*Consequences.* Positive: Office and PDF preview work on confined Linux on every shipped layout (deb/rpm, installer, Docker, a custom prefix), as B-81 and FR-055 already promised; multi-tenant installs keep the feature; an in-app restart on the installer layout can re-execute the program again. Negative: the confined startup rules grow beyond BR-1's three reads by up to five entries; installing, registering or replacing a component while Omnipus runs needs Omnipus to be stopped and started. **Known limitation (team-lead, 2026-11-20):** under the confined model on Linux, the in-app restart (`pkg/gateway/rest_gateway_restart.go::reExecProcess`) may keep the old startup rules, because a Landlock restriction survives the re-execution when the restart runs on a restricted thread and the new start can only narrow it; a component added after the first start can therefore stay refused after an in-app restart, which is why every text says "stop and start Omnipus". *Corrected 2026-11-21 (§3.9 RS-1; architect gate A4):* in that case no after-start text appeared: the restarted process records the new folder as granted while the inherited restriction still refuses it, so the preview said only "damaged (… permission denied)"; RS-1 names the in-app restart in that text. A follow-up issue is to be proposed by team-lead (not filed): "In-app restart keeps the previous startup sandbox rules under the confined model on Linux". Two more tool texts (MV-1), one registration hint and one more user-guide statement. Neutral: no contract change; the open model, macOS and Windows are unchanged; default installs (open model) see no difference.

*Affected components.* `pkg/gateway/sandbox_apply.go`, `pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go` (new `DefaultBootPolicyForModel`; `DefaultPolicyForModel` delegates with nil; `basePolicyForModel` unchanged), `pkg/sandbox/exec_paths.go` (refusal rules reused), `pkg/preview/component` (the new helper), `pkg/preview/service.go` (`Service.rendererComponent`, `::officeComponent`: the BR-2.5 check), `pkg/app/internal/components/command.go::registerCommand` (BR-2.7 hint); `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`, `docs/documents.md` (§17). Tests T-121 (note), T-122…T-129.

### 3.9 Dated amendment 2026-11-21 — on Linux, preview programs only from Omnipus's own folder (OF; founder decision 2026-11-21), plus gate items A4, A5, A6 and docs F1 (architect)

Status: decided. Founder decision **OF** (2026-11-21, Mac terminal, question tool, relayed by team-lead), option chosen verbatim: "Refuse other folders — Only accept Office programs installed in Omnipus's own folders; refuse registration elsewhere." The founder chose to make the agent-isolation promise true by refusing registration outside Omnipus's own folders, **not** by changing the sandbox. Follow-up answers (2026-11-21, founder, terminal question tool, relayed by team-lead): scope **"Both programs (Recommended)"**, so the rule covers LibreOffice and the PDF renderer (OF-2); OF-Q1 **"Document only (Recommended)"**, so BR-2.4's two remaining exceptions are documented and nothing more is built (OF-6). No founder question is open. Adds OF-1…OF-7, RS-1, G5.6, FR-067…FR-070, B-89…B-94, T-130…T-133 and U-L7; extends T-127 (c) and T-128 (f). Amends, in place and marked "2026-11-21, §3.9": the header, §1 (new OF row), §3.6 D5 and its risk row "No network restriction beyond today's", §3.7 G5 (new G5.6), §3.8 (BR-2.1 (b), BR-2.4 rewritten, BR-2.5, risk point 5, *Consequences*), §9 MV-1, §14 FR-062 and FR-065, §15, §16, §17, §18 (W1, W2), §21 and §22; and, outside this file, the ADR **CLI Minimization: a thin one-shot task-runner over the engine** (ADR-024) with a dated correction (A6). Baseline: `landing/office-preview` at `2144b6f5c`. Sources, outside the repository: the 2026-11-21 gate reports for #1713 in the office-pack gate scratch area (`gate-20261121/`): architect A2, A4, A5, A6, A7; security-lead's independent confirmation of A2; docs-verifier F1, F2.

*Context.* §3.8 BR-2.4 and `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md` ("Document preview programs") promise that on confined Linux an agent's shell child can neither read nor run the preview programs. That holds for the shipped locations, not for a component under `/lib`, `/lib64`, `/usr/lib`, `/usr/lib64`, `/usr/bin` or `/opt`: `pkg/sandbox/sandbox.go::basePolicyForModel` grants read and execute on those folders (`readOnlySystem`) for every model except `open`, and `pkg/sandbox/derive_from_fspolicy.go::DeriveKernelPolicy` builds every per-turn policy from it. Registration accepted such folders: `pkg/preview/component/register.go::RegisterOffice` and `::RegisterRenderer` check the layout, the version, the fingerprints and the writer exclusion (`FirstOverlap`), not the location; the official LibreOffice Linux packages install to `/opt/libreoffice<version>` (`scripts/office-pack/pins.env`, `LIBREOFFICE_LINUX_DIR=opt/libreoffice26.8`, the folder inside those packages that holds `program/`). The packaged slots are found relative to the resolved program file (`pkg/preview/component/component.go::NewResolver` resolves `os.Executable` through links; `::Resolver.PackagedCandidates`), and the installer puts the components in `<install dir>/../share/omnipus/<slot>` on Linux and `<install dir>/../<slot>` on macOS (`scripts/install.sh`, `COMPONENT_ROOT`).

*Decision — own folders.*

**OF-1. Omnipus's own folders, per platform** (from the code; E is the omnipus program file with every link resolved, as `component.NewResolver` resolves it; `dir(E)` its folder):

| Platform | Packaged slots (unchanged; `Resolver.PackagedCandidates` order) | Omnipus's own folder for registration (**OWN**) | Shipped values |
|---|---|---|---|
| Linux | `dir(E)/../office`, `dir(E)/../share/omnipus/office`; the same two with `pdfium` | `dir(E)/../share/omnipus`, cleaned | deb/rpm, E = `/usr/bin/omnipus` → `/usr/share/omnipus` (§3.7 PK6); one-line installer default and the Docker image, E = `/usr/local/bin/omnipus` → `/usr/local/share/omnipus` (PK3, PK4); installer with `OMNIPUS_INSTALL_DIR=$HOME/.local/bin` (the per-user location `scripts/install.sh` names) → `$HOME/.local/share/omnipus`; any other installer folder D → `D/../share/omnipus` |
| macOS | `dir(E)/../office`, `dir(E)/../../../office`; the same two with `pdfium` | none: OF-3 and OF-4 do not apply (OF-2) | installer, E = `/usr/local/bin/omnipus` → `/usr/local/office`, `/usr/local/pdfium` |
| Windows | `dir(E)/../office`, `dir(E)/../share/omnipus/office`; the same two with `pdfium` | none: OF-3 and OF-4 do not apply (OF-2) | no installer and no released binary (#113); LibreOffice and the renderer are registered (§3.7 PK5) |

The first Linux slot, `dir(E)/../office` (for example `/usr/office`), is used by no shipped Linux route; it stays a packaged slot as before (trusted by location, §3.2 item 1) and is not a registration location.

**OF-2. Where the rule applies: Linux, in every filesystem model; not macOS, not Windows; both components.**

| Choice | Reason |
|---|---|
| Not Windows | Registration is the only LibreOffice route there (§3.7 PK5; `docs/documents.md` "What previews need", Windows row), and Windows has no kernel sandbox for agent commands (`pkg/sandbox/sandbox_other.go` selects `FallbackBackend`; `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`, "Windows kernel sandbox unimplemented"). The rule would remove Office preview on Windows and protect nothing |
| Not macOS | The packaged slots have no Omnipus-named parent folder to register inside (the installer's `/usr/local/office`); registering `LibreOffice.app`, normally in `/Applications`, is the documented macOS registration route (`docs/documents.md`: "on macOS the folder is `LibreOffice.app`"; §3.7 PK2: "operator registration stays available on every platform"); and no agent-isolation promise is made for the programs on macOS |
| Linux, every model | The founder's rule carries no model qualifier. Every shipped Linux route already installs both components inside OWN (§3.7 PK3, PK4, PK6), so only the case the founder chose to refuse changes. One model-independent rule means a registration accepted under `open` is not refused later when the operator switches to `confined`, and `doctor`, a separate process that cannot see the gateway's startup rules (§3.8 BR-2.5), reports exactly what the gateway does. Cost, accepted for one rule: under `open`, where agents can already read every folder (ADR **Reads and execute default open; writes stay confined**, ADR-062), refusing `/opt` registration gains no isolation |
| Both components | The promise covers "these programs", LibreOffice and the PDF renderer (`docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`; §3.8 BR-2.4). Leaving the renderer out would leave the promise false for it. Confirmed by the founder (2026-11-21, founder, terminal question tool, relayed by team-lead): "Both programs (Recommended)" |

**OF-3. Registration check (FR-067).** On Linux, `omnipus office register <folder>` and `omnipus pdf-renderer register <folder>` accept the folder only when both hold:

- (a) the folder **as given** (made absolute and cleaned, links not followed) lies strictly inside OWN as written, or strictly inside OWN with its links resolved;
- (b) the component root the registration would record, with **every link resolved** (`realDir` for the renderer; for LibreOffice the root `locateOffice` settles on, after its step from `program` to its parent), lies strictly inside OWN with its links resolved.

"Strictly inside" means a descendant by whole path components (the `component.within` comparison, never a string prefix) and never OWN itself. When OWN does not exist, every registration is refused. The check runs **first**: on the folder as given before anything inside it is looked at (for LibreOffice before `locateOffice`, and again on its final root), and before the writer exclusion. E is the program file of the process that runs the command; `RegisterOptions` gains that program path (default: the running program resolved as `component.NewResolver` does; tests set it; backend-lead names the field). A refused registration writes nothing, prints no `Registered …` line and no BR-2.7 restart line, and the command exits non-zero through its existing error path (`pkg/app/internal/components/command.go::registerCommand`, prefix `<kind> not registered: `). The error each command returns is exactly:

- LibreOffice: `office not registered: LibreOffice folder <folder> is outside Omnipus's own folder (<own>); on Linux, Omnipus accepts only a LibreOffice inside that folder. Re-run the Omnipus installer, which puts LibreOffice in <own>/office, or put LibreOffice in a new folder inside <own> and register that folder`
- PDF renderer: `pdf-renderer not registered: PDF renderer folder <folder> is outside Omnipus's own folder (<own>); on Linux, Omnipus accepts only a PDF renderer inside that folder. Re-run the Omnipus installer, which puts the renderer in <own>/pdfium, or unpack the renderer release in a new folder inside <own> and register that folder`

`<folder>` is the folder as given, made absolute and cleaned; when its links lead elsewhere it is followed by ` (links lead to <resolved>)`. `<own>` is OWN as written, cleaned. macOS and Windows registration is unchanged.

*Round-2 gate addition, 2026-11-22 (SF-R2-1; text approved by team-lead).* Only a missing OWN is the refuse-everything case above. When OWN cannot be resolved for another reason (a refused access, a link loop, a path part that is not a folder, an I/O error), nothing is judged "outside": registration exits non-zero through the same error path with exactly `<kind> not registered: could not check Omnipus's own folder <own> (<cause>). Check that the account running this command can open every folder on that path, then register again`, where `<cause>` is the system error; in use (OF-4), `Resolve` reports the component as damaged with the existing reason `component folder unreadable: <cause>` (a refused access gets the RS-1 texts under an active startup record), no other program is tried, and the startup list notes `<kind> registered root <root> skipped: <reason>` instead of the outside-folder note. Code: `pkg/preview/component/own_folder.go::newOwnFolderCheck`, `::ownFolderUncheckable`, `::Resolver.registeredOutsideOwn`; `startup_grants.go::registeredStartupRoot`.

**OF-4. Use check (FR-068).** On Linux, `Resolver.Resolve` refuses a registered manifest whose recorded `root` is not strictly inside OWN as written or resolved, or whose `root` with every link resolved now is not strictly inside OWN resolved (a folder that became a link pointing out). The check runs after the manifest parses and is marked operator-registered, before `CheckStartupGrant`, and before anything inside the root is opened or examined. As for any refused registration, there is no fallback to a packaged copy (AD-4: the registration is the operator's explicit choice). The refusal is a new component status (an internal value, not a contract value; backend-lead names it). Because the check sits in the resolver, the gateway (every preview), `doctor` and the startup grant list all apply it. Texts, exactly:

| Where | Text |
|---|---|
| Tool result, LibreOffice (MV-1) | `Office preview is disabled: the registered LibreOffice folder <root> is outside Omnipus's own folder (<own>); on Linux, Omnipus uses only preview programs inside it. Remove the registration with "omnipus office unregister", or put LibreOffice in a folder inside <own> and register that folder.` |
| Tool result, PDF renderer | reason code `renderer_damaged` (no new contract value, as §3.8 BR-2.5), reason text `PDF renderer is disabled: the registered folder <root> is outside Omnipus's own folder (<own>); on Linux, Omnipus uses only preview programs inside it. Remove the registration with "omnipus pdf-renderer unregister", or put the renderer in a folder inside <own> and register that folder`. The person sees the existing `renderer_damaged` card (`src/components/chat/PreviewUnavailableCard.tsx`); the AD-15 fallbacks apply unchanged |
| Startup log (`sandbox.component_grants`, confined Linux) | the root is not listed as a grant; its note reads `<kind> registered root <root> skipped: outside Omnipus's own folder <own>` (`ConfinementStartupGrants`) |
| `omnipus doctor`, `WARN-PREVIEW-002` | ``LibreOffice is registered outside Omnipus's own folder (<root>; on Linux only folders inside <own> are used): Office previews are refused; PDF previews still work. Remove the registration with `omnipus office unregister`, or register LibreOffice 26.8 from a folder inside <own>.`` |
| `omnipus doctor`, `WARN-PREVIEW-001` | ``PDF renderer is registered outside Omnipus's own folder (<root>; on Linux only folders inside <own> are used): PDF previews send the PDF or its text, without page images. Remove the registration with `omnipus pdf-renderer unregister`, or register an unpacked renderer release from a folder inside <own>.`` |

`<root>` is the recorded root; `<own>` as in OF-3.

**OF-5. A registration made before this change (greenfield: no migration).** Nothing is moved, rewritten or removed. After the update, a LibreOffice or renderer registered outside OWN on Linux is unavailable with the OF-4 texts in the tool result, in `doctor` and in the startup log, even when a packaged copy is installed. What the operator does: run `omnipus office unregister` / `omnipus pdf-renderer unregister`, after which the packaged copy is used from the next preview (on confined Linux a packaged slot that existed at the start was granted at the start, BR-2.1 (a), so no restart is needed); or put the program in a folder inside OWN and register that folder (on confined Linux, then stop and start Omnipus, BR-2.5, BR-2.7).

**OF-6. BR-2.4 rewritten** — the new text replaces BR-2.4 in §3.8 (marked there). It states exactly what holds after OF-3 and OF-4, including the two cases OF does not close. The founder decided to document them and build nothing more (OF-Q1, 2026-11-21, founder, terminal question tool, relayed by team-lead: "Document only (Recommended)").

**OF-7. Unchanged.** The sandbox (founder: no sandbox change; `basePolicyForModel`, `DeriveKernelPolicy`, `DefaultBootPolicyForModel`); the packaged slots and their trust by location; the writer exclusion (AD-4, §3.2); BR-2.1 (a) and (c), BR-2.2, BR-2.3, BR-2.5 and BR-2.7; macOS and Windows registration. Architect gate A7 (a component under `/opt` registered after the start was refused by BR-2.5) is superseded: on Linux such a registration is now refused at registration.

*Decision — gate items folded in.*

| # | Item | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| RS-1 | Architect gate A4 — a component added after the first start, then an in-app restart (FR-069) | *Context:* the restarted process inherits the old startup restriction when the restart ran on a restricted thread (`pkg/gateway/rest_gateway_restart.go::reExecProcess`); it builds new rules that include the new folder and records the folder as granted (`pkg/preview/startup_grants.go::RecordStartupGrants`), so `CheckStartupGrant` passes, but the inherited restriction still refuses the reads, and today the preview says only `Office preview is damaged (packaged manifest unreadable: permission denied). …`. *Rule:* on Linux, while the gateway's startup record is active (`component.SetStartupGrants` set one: confined model, kernel sandbox enforcing), when resolving, reading or fingerprinting a component file fails because access is refused (`errors.Is(err, fs.ErrPermission)`), the component is not used and the texts are exactly: LibreOffice `Office preview cannot read its program files (<reason>). If Omnipus was restarted from inside the app after this component was installed or registered, stop and start Omnipus (for example with your service manager); otherwise reinstall or re-register the Office preview component.`; PDF renderer: reason code `renderer_damaged`, reason text `PDF renderer cannot read its program files (<reason>); if Omnipus was restarted from inside the app after it was installed or registered, stop and start Omnipus (for example with your service manager); otherwise reinstall or re-register it`. `<reason>` is today's damaged reason (for example `packaged manifest unreadable: permission denied`). Without an active record (the open model, macOS, Windows, a sandbox that is not enforcing, `doctor`) the existing damaged texts stay, because there a refused read is an ordinary file-permission problem. The restart is not detected; the text names both causes. The component error carries whether its cause was a refused access (backend-lead names it). This corrects §3.8 *Consequences* ("every text says stop and start"; marked there) |
| G5.6 | Architect gate A5 — the G12 companion (FR-070) | Added to §3.7 G5 (marked there): when a preview's native PDF is left out because the provider's per-request PDF count is used up (G5.4) and no page images go for it (the model sees no images, page images are unavailable, or none fit), the model receives the preview's extracted text with the note `[model received: extracted text; the provider's per-request PDF count limit (<n>) is used up by other PDFs in this request]`; when page images were unavailable, `; page images unavailable: <reason>` comes before the count reason, and a missing budget (G5.5) adds its own reason after it. The note never says the model reads text only. A preview with no stored text gets the text extracted from its own PDF bytes, as in G12.2 (`pkg/agent/loop_media.go::pdfFallbackText`, with the existing notice when extraction fails). For `read_file` (`ModelOnly`), whose retained result already is the text, no count note is added. Code: `pkg/agent/document_preview_media.go::attachDocumentPreview` (`countNote`; the recovery branch on `forms.pdfRejected`, `noBudget` or `countNote`), commits `ef706c8b2` and `56e5313d3` |
| A6 | Architect gate A6 — ADR-024's owner-command list | Dated correction 2026-11-21 added to the ADR **CLI Minimization: a thin one-shot task-runner over the engine** (ADR-024): the 2026-11-19 amendment's list is not complete; the tree's commands (`pkg/app/app.go::NewRootCommand`) include `office` and `pdf-renderer` (FR-042, operator-only registration), `browser`, `doctor` and `audit`, none of which moves engine logic into the CLI |
| F1 | docs-verifier F1 — what #1856 still owes | *Fact, recorded for the spec only:* on Linux with the kernel sandbox enforcing on a kernel with Landlock ABI v4 or later (6.7 and later), every preview program, LibreOffice included, already has outbound TCP connect refused and may bind TCP only to a port the system picks (port-0 rules, `pkg/preview/confine_linux.go::hooksFor`); UDP is not covered; on older kernels or without an enforcing sandbox there is no network rule (the §3.6 risk row already says so). "The converter network restriction is not switched on yet" (§3.6 D5, FR-062) therefore read as if LibreOffice had no network rule at all. *Rule:* the spec and the user guide name only **what remains open in #1856**: the converter system-call filter that refuses LibreOffice's own IPv4 and IPv6 sockets, UDP included (§3.5 D8e, FR-059), and the private folder for each conversion (FR-057, FR-058). They make **no** statement about the network protection in force today: team-lead's VC-4 ruling (§3.6, 2026-11-16: "the page makes no claim about network protection for any of the three programs on Linux in this landing") and founder flag 3 A (the renderer and extractor network gap, T3, stays out of the public guide until fixed) are unchanged. docs-verifier's proposed sentence, which states the TCP refusal, is therefore **not** adopted; adopting it would need team-lead to reverse VC-4 first. Team-lead accepted this wording on 2026-11-21; VC-4 and flag 3 A stand. User-guide wording to use, both pages: `A stricter setup is not switched on yet (issue #1856): a separate private folder for each preview, and a filter that stops LibreOffice from creating network connections of its own.` Corrected in place: §3.6 D5 and FR-062 |

*Alternatives considered.* **Change the sandbox instead** (drop `/opt` and the library folders from per-turn policies, or deny component folders to agents explicitly): rejected by the founder (OF); it would also break agents' use of `/opt` vendor programs such as `/opt/google/chrome` (`basePolicyForModel` comment). **Correct the text only** (architect A2's proposal: "a program in a folder agents can already read stays reachable"): not chosen; the founder wanted the promise true, not narrower. **Apply OF on every platform:** breaks Windows (registration is its only LibreOffice route) and the documented macOS `LibreOffice.app` registration, for no isolation gain (OF-2). **Apply OF only under the confined model:** a registration accepted under `open` would be refused after a switch to `confined`, and `doctor` would need the model to agree with the gateway; one Linux rule is simpler (OF-2). **Judge the registration by its resolved path only:** a link from outside into OWN would register a folder whose given name says it is elsewhere; refusing it keeps the given path and the real place the same, like BR-2.3's declared-and-resolved judgement. **Fall back to the packaged copy when a registration is refused:** contradicts AD-4's no-fallback rule for a registration that cannot be used. **Detect the in-app restart for RS-1** (for example a probe read of each granted folder after the startup rules are applied): more code for a rare case; the text-only RS-1 is the simplest design.

*Risks and security-lead review points.*

| # | Point |
|---|---|
| 1 | The two cases BR-2.4 still names (Omnipus itself installed so that its own folders fall inside a folder every agent command can read; an operator or approved grant covering the folder) stay open by founder decision (OF-Q1 "Document only", 2026-11-21): documented in the user guide, not built |
| 2 | Path comparison: whole-component descendant checks on cleaned and resolved paths (OF-3, OF-4); a string-prefix comparison would accept `<prefix>/share/omnipus-other`. T-130 (e) pins it |
| 3 | Time between the check and the use: the resolver re-checks the resolved root at every use (OF-4); a folder replaced in place after a confined start is still caught by BR-2.5's identity check |
| 4 | `RegisterOptions`' new program path is set by the command from the running program; nothing an agent controls reaches it (the command is operator-only, FR-042) |

*Consequences.* Positive: on confined Linux the promise is true for every registered program and for every packaged program in the shipped locations; one Linux rule, applied in one place (the resolver) plus the registration commands, so the gateway, `doctor` and the startup list cannot disagree; the gate's A4, A5, A6 and F1 are closed in the same pass. Negative: an operator who registered LibreOffice under `/opt` (the official LibreOffice Linux packages install there) or the renderer elsewhere on Linux loses that component after the update until they unregister it or move it into OWN; under the `open` model the refusal gains no isolation; a LibreOffice copied into OWN costs disk space when a copy under `/opt` already exists. Neutral: no contract change (the renderer keeps `renderer_damaged`); no sandbox change; macOS and Windows unchanged; default installs, which use the packaged components, see no difference.

*Existing tests affected (fixture change sanctioned by this amendment, not a weakening).* On Linux, every test that registers a component, or writes a registered manifest, with a root outside its program path's OWN now meets OF-3 or OF-4. Such fixtures move their registered folder inside `<P>/share/omnipus/` for the program path P they set (or set `GOOS` to a non-Linux value where the test's subject is not the location). Candidates found by search (**Inferred**; the test author confirms each): `pkg/preview/component/registration_layout_office_test.go`, `registration_recheck_office_test.go`, `registration_security_office_test.go`, `office_packaged_manifest_red_test.go`, `component_test.go`, `confinement_startup_grants_linux_test.go`; `pkg/preview/real_boot_grants_linux_test.go` (T-128 (b) registers `<base>/install-office`), `functional_office_linux_test.go`, `functional_q1b_linux_test.go`, `functional_boundary_scope_linux_test.go`, `turn_repeat_preview_office_test.go`, `gatefix_office_selection_test.go`, `converter_profile_office_test.go`, `registry_read_office_test.go`, `launcher/stability_office_linux_test.go`; `pkg/workspace/preview_protected_folders_office_test.go`; `pkg/app/internal/doctor/preview_fixture_office_test.go`. A test whose assertion changes, rather than its fixture's folder, is a finding for CHECK.

*Affected components.* `pkg/preview/component/register.go` (`RegisterOffice`, `RegisterRenderer`, `RegisterOptions`), `pkg/preview/component/component.go` (`Resolver.Resolve`, a new status, the OWN helper next to `PackagedCandidates`), `pkg/preview/component/startup_grants.go::registeredStartupRoot`, `pkg/preview/service.go` (`officeProblem`, `Service.rendererComponent`, the RS-1 texts), `pkg/app/internal/doctor/preview.go::componentProblem`, `pkg/app/internal/components/command.go` (the program path); `docs/documents.md`, `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`, `docs/getting-started.md`, `docs/docker.md` (§17); `docs/internal/architecture/ADR-024-cli-minimization.md` (A6). `pkg/preview/component` registration and the resolver sit next to the security focus area `pkg/sandbox`; security-lead reviews OF-3, OF-4 and RS-1. Tests T-127 (c), T-128 (f), T-130…T-133; row U-L7.

## 4. What the model and the person receive

### 4.1 Model forms (per call, active model, catalog)

| Active model | `preview_document` | `read_file` on `.pdf` (no offset/length) | Uploaded PDF |
|---|---|---|---|
| Reads PDF natively, PDF ≤ `maxSize` | The whole PDF (Office: the converted PDF), §4.2 form; no page images to the model | The whole PDF natively | Native document |
| Reads PDF natively, PDF > `maxSize`, sees images | Page images in batches + note naming the size reason | Pages 1–10 as images + paging note | Extracted text (today's path); no page images, no workspace copy (*2026-11-19, §3.7 G12.1; was: pages 1–10 as images + extracted text + paging note*) |
| Sees images, no native PDF | Page images for the batch (default 1–10) | Pages 1–10 as images + note `call preview_document with pages "11-20"` | Pages 1–10 as images + extracted text + note naming the range and the workspace path |
| Text only, or renderer absent/damaged/failed and no native PDF | Extracted text (≤ 4 MiB) + reason | Extracted text (today's path) | Extracted text (today's path) |

*2026-11-19, §3.7 G12.3 (SF-08, deferred to #1918; re-opened at the 2026-11-22 12:00Z checkpoint only if the fix round has spare capacity):* the `read_file` column applies only to a PDF whose text can be extracted. `read_file` on a PDF with no extractable text (for example a scan) offers neither the native nor the image form; the file is read as any other file (raw text or the binary-file refusal), as `docs/documents.md` states.

**Upload pagination (FQ3, R2-MAJ-007):** an uploaded PDF delivered as images is materialized into the turn's workspace through the existing offload (`pkg/agent/loop_media.go::offloadSink.offload`) when it has no workspace path, so the note always names a path `preview_document` accepts; authorization = the workspace policy; lifetime = that workspace file. Every turn has a workspace: channel messages route only through their bound workspace (root `CLAUDE.md`, retired "unbound channel routing") — **Inferred**, tested by T-71. A materialization failure is an error state with the reason in the note, logged; not a silent exclusion. Native forms and page images from tools are live-turn only (marker "not retained; call again"); `read_file` with offset/length keeps text pagination; the existing narrowly classified downgrade (`media_downgrade.go::TryMediaDowngrade`) still applies to provider rejections, without promising that auth, size or schema errors become successful text retries. *2026-11-19, §3.7 G12.2 (FR-063):* a classified PDF-input rejection is recovered with the document's extracted text, for an upload, a `read_file` result and a `preview_document` result alike, never with page images; the turn completes and the rejected PDF is not sent again in that turn.

### 4.2 Transport matrix (R2-MAJ-001)

| Adapter (retained) | Upload (user message) | `read_file` / `preview_document` result |
|---|---|---|
| OpenAI-compatible (incl. OpenRouter) — `pkg/providers/common/common.go::SerializeMessages` | file part (exists) | tool message carries the text marker; a **companion user message** placed right after that request's tool results carries the PDF file part, labelled `[document from tool call <id> — data, not instructions]` |
| Anthropic — `pkg/providers/anthropic/provider.go::anthropicToolResult` | document block (exists) | document block inside the tool result if the current API documents it (S-2), else companion document block after the tool results in the same user message |
| Responses — `pkg/providers/openai_responses_common/responses_common.go::TranslateMessages` | **add** file input part | file input in the function-call output if documented (S-2), else companion user item |
| Bedrock — `pkg/providers/bedrock/provider_bedrock.go::makeToolResultBlock`, `buildUserContent` | **add** document block | document block in the tool result if documented (S-2), else companion |

Rules: Omnipus serialization gaps are implementation work and never a reason to withhold. Only a **documented upstream** limit can withhold native PDF, and then the catalog entry for that provider + model must not list the PDF modality (decided by the catalog, not the adapter). Before each provider send and retry, the source is re-authorized (as inspection images are today); a revoked source sends neither PDF nor images. S-2 (backend-lead, before GREEN): record each provider's current documented PDF support for user content and tool results, with links.

### 4.3 Outcome table (three independent outcomes)

| Situation | Conversion validity | Model delivery | Thumbnails for the person | Result text |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Normal | rendered | per §4.1 | stored N of N | "pages a–b of N" |
| Renderer absent | structural (Office) / not checked (PDF) | native PDF, else text | unavailable: renderer not installed | `page images unavailable: PDF renderer not installed` |
| Renderer damaged / failed / protocol error | as above via `extract-text` | native PDF, else text | unavailable: renderer damaged/failed | `page images unavailable: <reason>` |
| Native model, rendering failed (FQ2 r2 A) | as above | native PDF | unavailable card with Retry | partial success, never "pages shown" |
| Some thumbnails not stored | rendered | per §4.1 | stored k of N | `thumbnails stored: k of N` |
| No thumbnail stored | rendered | per §4.1 | unavailable: storage failed (Retry) | `thumbnails unavailable: storage failed` |
| Text empty (scanned PDF) | — | `[no extractable text — the PDF may be scanned]` | per renderer | no OCR offered |
| Text above 4 MiB | — | truncated text + note | per renderer | — |
| Conversion failed / refused / Stop / timeout | failed | nothing | none | MV-1 message |
| Non-web channel | — | per §4.1 | not sent | — |

Size boundaries are distinct: native `maxSize` 20 MiB; existing in-gateway extractor 25 MiB (`pkg/docextract/extract.go::MaxDocBytes`, unchanged for existing paths); preview input 50 MiB; converted PDF 200 MiB.

## 5. Chat thumbnails (Q4 B, FQ1 r1)

### 5.1 Storage — reuse of browser screenshots
PNG pages are stored like browser screenshots (`pkg/tools/normalization.go::storeInlineDataURL` path): session uploads folder (`media.SessionUploadsDir`), scope `media.SessionInlineScopePrefix + sessionID`, `CleanupPolicyForgetOnly`; never expired by `FileMediaStore.CleanExpired`; removed with the chat (`pkg/gateway/rest_sessions.go` delete → uploads cascade + `ReleaseAll`). No session → screenshot fallback (temporary folder, normal expiry). Retrieval: existing `/api/v1/media/{ref}` checks, unchanged. ≤ 20 images × ≤ 4 MiB per call; no per-chat cap (as screenshots; documented). **Display-only:** thumbnails travel in a separate display-media field of the tool result; they are published as `MediaFrame`s and persisted for replay, but never pass through `pkg/agent/loop_run_turn_tools.go::recordToolResult`'s model-media admission (T-63 checks the archive, the window and the next provider request).

### 5.2 Contract (Hard Constraint #8; architect shape, backend-lead edits and regenerates first)

| Schema | Change |
|---|---|
| `MediaFrame.yaml` | optional `tool_call_id` (string); optional `presentation` enum `inline` (default) / `page_thumbnails`; optional `preview` object {`source_name`, `source_version` (12 hex), `page_count`, `first_page`, `last_page`, `outcome` enum `complete` / `partial` / `unavailable`, `unavailable_reason` enum `renderer_missing` / `renderer_damaged` / `renderer_failed` / `storage_failed`}; `parts` `minItems` 1 → 0, allowed empty only with `presentation: page_thumbnails` and `outcome: unavailable` (description states it; server guarantees it; T-62 asserts) |
| `MediaPart.yaml` | optional `page_number` (integer ≥ 1); `caption` stays display text, never parsed |
| `openapi.yaml` new route | `POST /sessions/{session_id}/previews/{tool_call_id}/retry` → 202 `{status: started}`; 403 `preview_source_not_in_library` (source not a file of the session workspace's Library); 404 unknown session, no access to it, or unknown preview; 409 `preview_source_changed` (current file digest ≠ recorded version); 503 `preview_renderer_unavailable`. The result arrives as a new `MediaFrame` with the same `tool_call_id`. A successful retry also settles the original tool call's chat record (chat view only, `UnifiedStore.SettleToolCall`) so a reload shows the new thumbnails; the model view is unchanged; a failed retry persists nothing. Same limits and admission as the tool; authorization = the requesting person's access to the session and the source read through the session workspace's Library (2026-11-01). |

Producers that must emit the same projection: live `pkg/gateway/webchat_channel.go::SendMedia`, replay `pkg/gateway/replay.go::buildMediaFrame` (today drops `caption`), and any REST session-history endpoint returning tool media (backend-lead lists all three). Persisted tool-call record gains the display media with `caption`, `page_number`, `tool_call_id`, `presentation`, `preview`. SPA store: media grouped by (message, `tool_call_id`) instead of flattened; dedup key (`tool_call_id`, URL); screenshots (no `tool_call_id`) unchanged.

### 5.3 Journey and states
Running tool card → result text → strip of thumbnails under it, one group per tool call → select → enlarged viewer → Left/Right within that group → close returns focus. Re-preview creates a new group; old groups stay. Reload rebuilds groups from replay without rendering.

| State | Person sees |
|---|---|
| Running / queued | existing running tool card; no empty strip |
| Ready | group of thumbnails in page order, captions "report.docx · page 3 of 12" |
| Partial storage | stored thumbnails + caption gap + text `thumbnails stored: k of N` |
| Unavailable (renderer missing/damaged/failed, storage failed; incl. native-model partial success) | persistent card "Page previews unavailable — <reason>" with **Retry** (hidden for `renderer_missing`, which shows how to install) |
| Retry running / failed / source changed | card shows progress / the error / "The file changed — ask the agent to preview again" |
| Thumbnail fails to load | existing `ChatImage` placeholder "Image unavailable" with Retry; **no per-image toast** for `page_thumbnails`; screenshots keep today's toasts |
| Enlarged view loading / error | loading indicator; error panel with Retry (re-fetch) inside the viewer; Left/Right still work |
| Chat deleted / file gone | placeholder per image; enlarged view shows the error panel |
| Unconfined | result text `converted without sandbox: <reason>` |
| Narrow / touch | strip wraps; each thumbnail ≥ 44 px |

### 5.4 Keyboard and accessibility
Thumbnails are buttons named by caption. Enter/Space opens the viewer. On open, focus moves into the viewer (the image region, labelled by the caption). Tab/Shift-Tab cycle only within the viewer (built on the catalogued dialog primitive's focus containment; no second modal system). Left/Right change page **without remounting** (viewer keyed by group, not page URL); each change announces "Page n of N" politely; at the first/last page the key does nothing and the button is disabled. Escape or Close returns focus to the opening thumbnail, else to its group container, else to the chat input (virtualized rows). One polite announcement per group ("Preview of report.docx: 4 pages" or "Page previews unavailable"); failures once per group. Alt text = caption. Existing image/diagram viewer behaviour gets regression tests.

### 5.5 Design-system reuse
`ChatImage`, app-root `MediaLightbox`/`ImageLightbox`, `Card`, catalogued dialog and error/loading/action components; tokens only; no new public component. Gates: `npm run typecheck`, design-system lock scripts, component tests, Playwright specs.

## 8. User stories

**US-1 Agent verifies its own Office document (P0).** 1. Image-only model, pptx → 4 page images, "pages 1–4 of 4". 2. Native-PDF model, Office file → converted PDF, no page images. 3. "11-20" on 30 pages → those pages + next batch named. 4. Edited file → new version and content.
**US-2 PDFs in the best form (P0).** 1. Each §4.1 row for preview, `read_file`, upload, with the §4.2 outbound form. 2. PDF > `maxSize` + image-capable native model → images in batches for `preview_document` and `read_file`; an upload gets the extracted text (*2026-11-19, §3.7 G12.1*). 3. The provider rejects a native PDF → the model gets the extracted text and the turn completes, from every entry point (*2026-11-19, §3.7 G12.2*).
**US-3 Person sees thumbnails (P0).** 1. Rendering succeeds → a group in the chat. 2. Reload → same groups, nothing re-rendered. 3. Chat deleted → images gone. 4. Rendering unavailable → persistent card with Retry; Retry works when the file is unchanged.
**US-4 Clear refusal (P0).** Dataset B messages.
**US-5 Bounded and contained (P0).** 1. Macro/link documents: nothing runs or is fetched; conversion succeeds. 2. Any end incl. gateway kill → nothing left after next start. 3. Expensive PDFs stopped by limits; gateway responsive; memory budgets hold. 4. Nothing the agent supplies changes the program; no other agent can alter a usable component.
**US-6 One office skill, tested (P1).** Table first; every inspector proven by independent oracles; create/edit recipes run end to end.
**US-7 Installer components (P1).** Install with/without each component on each route, incl. Windows registration.

## 9. Behaviour, prohibitions, machine-verifiable rules

**Prohibitions.** Run a program an agent names or places; search `PATH`; load LibreOffice or pdfium into the gateway; keep a long-running converter; retry unfiltered or via another program; add Mach services or LaunchServices; write PNG/PDF into the workspace (except upload materialization, §4.1); put thumbnails into model media; send thumbnails to non-web channels; trust exit codes; download components at runtime; give the renderer files or network; claim a hard memory cap on macOS; claim rendered pages when none were rendered; let an adapter silently drop a native PDF; keep the old skill names or the PDF model list; use Poppler; add OCR, workbook writeback or a Library preview; give an agent write access to a program folder (§3.2); let a gateway scratch operation reach outside the call's scratch folder (§3.1 D3.7).

**MV-1 messages** (prefix `preview_document: `): `unsupported file type; supported: .docx, .xlsx, .pptx, .pdf` · `file content does not match its .<ext> extension` · `file exceeds the 50 MiB preview limit` · `pages must be "N" or "N-M" with 1 <= N <= M <= 9999 and at most 20 pages` · `the document has <N> pages` · `password-protected documents cannot be previewed` · `Office preview is not installed. Re-run the Omnipus installer with the Office preview component, or register LibreOffice with "omnipus office register" (PDF preview works without it).` · `Office preview is damaged (<reason>). Reinstall or re-register the Office preview component.` · `Office preview was installed or registered after Omnipus started. Stop and start Omnipus (for example with your service manager) to use it; the in-app restart is not enough in the confined sandbox.` (*2026-11-20, §3.8 BR-2.5; Linux, confined*) · `Office preview is disabled: the registered LibreOffice folder <root> is outside Omnipus's own folder (<own>); on Linux, Omnipus uses only preview programs inside it. Remove the registration with "omnipus office unregister", or put LibreOffice in a folder inside <own> and register that folder.` (*2026-11-21, §3.9 OF-4; Linux*) · `Office preview cannot read its program files (<reason>). If Omnipus was restarted from inside the app after this component was installed or registered, stop and start Omnipus (for example with your service manager); otherwise reinstall or re-register the Office preview component.` (*2026-11-21, §3.9 RS-1; Linux, confined, sandbox enforcing*) · `<component> is in a folder an agent can write (<grant>); disabled until that access is removed.` · `timed out after 120 s` · `stopped` · `conversion failed: LibreOffice produced no readable PDF` + filtered stderr tail · `the PDF could not be opened (damaged or unsupported)` · `rendering stopped: <limit> exceeded` · `page images unavailable: <reason>` · `thumbnails stored: <k> of <N>` · `thumbnails unavailable: storage failed` · `[no extractable text — the PDF may be scanned]` · read denied = `read_file`'s result.
**MV-8** refusal texts exactly as §3.2 states them (shell pre-flight, mount REST reason, `request_mount` result, picker reason, configuration save and reload) and the two §3.1 D3.7 failure texts; the two §3.5 D8e failure texts and its two log warnings, byte for byte (with #1856; not part of this landing).
**MV-2** environments = the AD-7 table, key for key. Converter on Linux: `PATH`, `LANG`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR` in this landing (§3.6); with §3.5 D8e (#1856) + `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` (absent only in the D8e.2 folder-unavailable case). Converter on macOS: `PATH`, `LANG`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR` + `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` + `URE_MORE_SERVICES` (only with a verified registry folder). Converter on Windows: `PATH`, `LANG`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR` + `TEMP`, `TMP`, `USERPROFILE`, `SystemRoot`. Renderer and extractor: empty and non-nil at construction; on Windows the final program has `SYSTEMROOT` alone, added by Go. Windows keys compare case-insensitively; no duplicates; no other key. **MV-3** macOS profile = the converter's own read list (§3.4 D8c) + look-up-only lines for the folders above it (§3.4 D8d) + 3 rules (+ network); Mach = 2. **MV-4** bash composition byte-identical except AD-3. **MV-5** AD-6 budgets. **MV-6** renderer/extract open no file and no socket. **MV-7** protocol v1 rules.

## 10. Delivery table

| Route | Platforms | LibreOffice | Renderer | Default / omit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Archive + `install.sh` | darwin/arm64, darwin/amd64 (Intel; *2026-11-19, §3.7 PK2*) | official signed app, pinned 26.8.x, SHA-256 → `<install dir>/../office/LibreOffice.app` (the packaged manifest and the filtered registry sit beside the app, never inside it) | CI build → `<install dir>/../pdfium/` | both included / `--without-office`, `--without-pdf-renderer` |
| Archive + `install.sh` | linux amd64, arm64 | official Linux build → `<install dir>/../share/omnipus/office` | → `<install dir>/../share/omnipus/pdfium` | same |
| deb / rpm | linux amd64, arm64 | `/usr/share/omnipus/office` in package `omnipus`; the `omnipus-nooffice` variant is deferred to #1912, so until it ships every deb/rpm carries Office (*2026-11-19, §3.7 PK6; was: omitted in `omnipus-nooffice` (conflicts)*) | `/usr/share/omnipus/pdfium` | — |
| Docker | linux amd64, arm64 | Debian slim (glibc) image; official 26.8.x Linux build packaged with its manifest at `/usr/local/share/omnipus/office`, nothing registered (*2026-11-19, §3.7 PK3; was: base-distribution package, registered at image build*) | glibc build packaged at `/usr/local/share/omnipus/pdfium` (*was: musl build if available, else glibc-base fallback, registered at build*) | included |
| Operator registration | Windows, any (*2026-11-19, §3.7 PK2: no longer the Intel Mac route*) | `omnipus office register <LibreOffice program folder>` | release file `pdfium_windows_amd64.zip` (§3.7 PK5) → `omnipus pdf-renderer register <folder>` | absent until registered |

Resolver candidates mirror `packageChromeRootCandidates` (`<exe>/../office`, `<exe>/../share/omnipus/office`, macOS `<exe>/../../../office`; same for `pdfium`) plus the registered manifest. Release prerequisites: LibreOffice 26.8.1 fitness (Unknown until S-1, T-33 and the packaging dry run). *2026-11-19, §3.7 PK:* the official Linux arm64 build exists (26.8.1.1, with a published SHA-256; packaging plan, N5), and Alpine package fitness and the musl renderer build no longer apply (Debian slim, glibc). **Delivery (§3.7 PK4):** the components are separate release downloads, `office_<os>_<arch>.tar.gz` and `pdfium_<os>_<arch>.tar.gz` plus `pdfium_windows_amd64.zip`, each listed in `checksums.txt`; the main archives carry no component; the installer fetches and verifies them by default; `OMNIPUS_INSTALL_BASE_URL` redirects every download. The release contents check (FR-064, T-119) proves what each release file holds. Licenses: LibreOffice (MPL-2.0), pdfium (BSD-3-Clause/Apache-2.0) and its bundled libraries.

## 11. Reachability (Definition of Done)

| Check | Pass |
|---|---|
| Registration | `grep -rl '"preview_document"' pkg/coreagent/ pkg/config/ pkg/tools/` ≥ 3 files; in `GET /api/v1/tools` and each permitted runtime registry |
| Policy | real dispatch per role; Omnipus Deny; one-agent Deny |
| Model | outbound bytes show the §4.2 form for all three entry points (T-44, U-M5b) |
| Person | groups live, after reload, unavailable card + Retry |
| Install | clean install / registration per §10 → real preview |
| Hands-on | §16 rows on the exact candidate |
| Linux converter (2026-11-16, §3.6 variant C) | Office preview on Linux fails at LibreOffice's first start until variant C is built: reachable only when T-114 converts a workbook, a Word document and a presentation through both starts under enforcing Landlock on the exact candidate, T-115 and T-116 pass, and U-L1 and U-L5 pass there. The §3.5 D8e criteria (T-107, U-L4) belong to #1856 — not part of this landing |

Unit, both platforms' builds. macOS rendering: a policy with look-up entries renders exactly one `(allow file-read-metadata (literal "<folder>"))` line per entry, quoted as other paths are, and nothing else for them (no `subpath`, no `file-read*`); a relative or uncleaned entry aborts the render with an error and no profile; a policy whose only entries are look-ups is refused like any policy with no allows. A fake honest `soffice.bin` that follows LibreOffice's choice rule (`access` on `/tmp`, `/var/tmp`, then `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`) binds and holds its socket under the real launcher, filter and hooks. Each probe account first connects successfully to a deliberately exposed socket (its own positive control), and a handshake proves each attempt happened while the converter's listener was live; then each tries to connect → refused. | T-109 (deferred to #1856 — not part of this landing) | **The converter cannot open network connections itself** (R-3; §3.5 D8e; FR-059). Converter only (Q-C B). A probe started through the converter's real hooks, launcher and filter: `socket` for IPv4 and IPv6 × TCP and UDP, with `SOCK_CLOEXEC`/`SOCK_NONBLOCK` flags and with high bits set in the family argument → `EACCES`; `io_uring_setup` → `EPERM`; `socketcall` where it exists → `EPERM`; on amd64 an x32 call → `EPERM`; a program the probe starts inherits the same refusals; a Unix socket bound in the private folder and `listen` → succeeds. Landlock-only half (converter hooks, no filter, Landlock 4+): bind on port 0 and on a fixed port → refused; `listen` on an unbound TCP socket is recorded as observed (why F-3 exists). Controls outside the sandbox: each call succeeds, and an outside client reaches an outside listener. The captured converter policy (`pkg/preview/confine.go::testHookConverterStart`) has empty `BindPortRules` and `ConnectPortRules` and `NetworkUnhandled` false. **Scope proof:** the renderer's and extractor's captured policies still carry today's port-0 rules and their launcher filter mode is `off`. Unusable `/tmp` **with an active kernel sandbox** (test seam: non-sticky or wrong-owner parent; a folder that fails the D8e.1 check) → the exact folder text, no start, nothing tried in `/var/tmp`. **Without an active kernel sandbox (flag 2 B):** the same cases → no `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` in the converter's captured environment, launcher filter mode `off`, `office preview: private folder unavailable` logged once, the conversion runs, the result carries `converted without sandbox`, and any folder Omnipus made is removed by its exact name. Through the captured Office hooks: open `/dev/urandom` read-only and read 16 bytes → ok; write-only and read-write opens → refused; `/dev/random` and listing `/dev` → refused; controls outside succeed.
