(e) A recorded root inside OWN that has become a link to a folder outside → refused as in (a). (f) After `Unregister` → the packaged component resolves `StatusReady`. (g) `GOOS` darwin and windows, same manifest with a readable root → no OF-4 refusal (guard). RED: (a)–(e) fail on an assertion on the code before §3.9 | Unit | B-92 |
| T-132 | **Refused read under the active startup record** (2026-11-21, §3.9 RS-1; FR-069). (a) Go Tests, Linux, non-root: `component.SetStartupGrants` with a record that grants the packaged slots (cleared in `t.Cleanup`); the slot's `manifest.json` mode 000 → Office tool result exactly `preview_document: ` + the RS-1 LibreOffice text, with `<reason>` beginning `packaged manifest unreadable:` and containing `permission denied`; the renderer: `renderer_damaged` and exactly the RS-1 renderer text. (b) Same files, no record set → today's texts exactly (`Office preview is damaged (…). Reinstall or re-register the Office preview component.`; `PDF renderer damaged (…)`), no restart sentence. (c) A refusal that is not a permission error under an active record (a fingerprint mismatch) → today's damaged text (guard). (d) The real case is T-128 (f). RED: (a) fails on an assertion on the code before §3.9 | Unit | B-93 |
| T-133 | **Count used up: text with the count note** (2026-11-21, §3.7 G5.6; FR-070). Existing Go Tests `pkg/agent/document_preview_gatefix_g12_test.go::TestOfficePreview_CountLimitNoteNamesTheCountNotTextOnly` and `::TestOfficePreview_CountLimitWithoutStoredTextSendsFallbackText` are this row, tightened to the exact note `[model received: extracted text; the provider's per-request PDF count limit (1) is used up by other PDFs in this request]` (with stored text: followed by that text; without: followed by the fallback extraction of the preview's own bytes); plus (c) a `ModelOnly` preview (the `read_file` path) under the same used-up count → no count note is added. The behaviour already exists (`ef706c8b2`, `56e5313d3`), so there is no RED on the pre-change code: the test author proves each assertion can fail with a mutation of `attachDocumentPreview` (for example dropping `countNote` from the recovery condition, or from `reason`) | Unit | B-94 |

**Linux converter rows (T-107…T-113, 2026-11-15, §3.5) — deferred to #1856, not part of this landing.** Runners: Go Tests = `test-shards` in `.github/workflows/pr.yml` (`ubuntu-latest`, kernel sandbox primed by `pkg/preview/confinement_prime_linux_test.go::primeKernelSandbox`); Office real = job `office-preview-real` ("Office preview real programs"), tests listed in `scripts/office-preview-real/real-tests.txt`, which fails on a skipped or BLOCKED test. T-107 and T-108 go into that list. Every refusal needs an outside-the-sandbox success control on the same object, so a refusal cannot pass because the target was missing. Red for T-108…T-113 must be a failing assertion, not a compile failure. Tests for T1 (a hostile permission change) and T2 (reused-name recovery) are written with those fixes, not in this round.

**Variant-C rows (T-114…T-118, 2026-11-16, §3.6).** Runners as above: T-114…T-116 go into `scripts/office-preview-real/real-tests.txt` and run in `office-preview-real`; T-118 runs in Go Tests; T-117 as stated. Every refusal needs an outside-the-sandbox success control on the same object. Red for T-115…T-118 must be a failing assertion, not a compile failure.

**2026-11-19 rows (T-119…T-121, §3.7).** T-119 runs in the release pipelines and the packaging dry run. T-120 runs in Go Tests; its red must be a failing assertion, not a compile failure. T-121's `pkg/sandbox` cases run in Go Tests; `TestPreviewOffice_Q1B_ForcedConfinedBootMatchesOpen` is listed in `scripts/office-preview-real/real-tests.txt` (job `office-preview-real`). T-119 and T-121 exist at `376c40e2d` or on the packaging branch; T-120 is new.

**2026-11-20 rows (T-122…T-129, §3.8).** T-122, T-123, T-124, T-125 (a) and T-129 run in Go Tests; T-125 (b), T-126 and T-128 go into `scripts/office-preview-real/real-tests.txt` and run in `office-preview-real`; T-127 runs in the packaging dry run. All are new; each red must be a failing assertion, not a compile failure (the new function and helper are introduced as stubs first if needed). Every refusal needs an outside-the-sandbox success control on the same object.

**Windows inventory (T-41):** `TestPreviewDocument_Windows*`, `TestRunLimited_WindowsJob*`, `TestPreviewRecovery_Windows*`, `TestOfficeRegister_Windows*`, `TestInspectors_Windows*` and the renderer's own smoke/limits/cancel tests built from the C sources; the receipt step lists every expected test name and fails on a missing, skipped or failed one.

**Datasets.** A (valid): A1 FX-PPTX4 → 4; A2 FX-DOCX6 → 6; A3 FX-XLSX3 → 3; A4 FX-PDF30 "11-20" → 10; A5 "3" → 1; A6 default → 10 of 30; A7 non-ASCII name; A8 50 MiB exactly; A9 "1-1"; A10 FX-PDF-FORM. B (refusals/security): R1 `.txt`; R2 PNG as `.docx`; R3 50 MiB + 1; R4 "0", "5-3", "1-21", "a", "10000"; R5 "40" of 30; R6 encrypted; R7 truncated; R8 `.docm` `.doc` `.odt`; R9 outside policy / symlink to a secret folder; S1 FX-MACRO; S2 FX-LINK; S3 hang + grandchild; S4 junk + `%%EOF`; S5 fake soffice; S6 replaced executable; S7 root in a writable mount (own and other agent); S8 FX-BOMB; S9 renderer replaced; S10 renderer absent; S11 malformed frames; S12 PID reuse. Sizes: 20 MiB ± 1 (native), 25 MiB ± 1 (existing extractor, existing paths), 50 MiB ± 1 (input), 200 MiB ± 1 (converted).
**Regression:** `read_file` text pagination and PNG/JPEG inspection; existing upload text path; bash composition except AD-3; Mach set; screenshot storage/replay (plus caption persistence); existing image/diagram viewer.

## 14. Requirements

| ID | Requirement |
|---|---|
| FR-001 | MUST provide `preview_document(file, pages?)` per AD-1/AD-5. |
| FR-002 | MUST accept only the four types, matching content, ≤ 50 MiB, unencrypted. |
| FR-003 | MUST read the source only via the caller's read authorization, audited like `read_file`; convert a scratch copy. |
| FR-004 | MUST apply AD-5 page rules and name the next batch. |
| FR-005 | MUST deliver to the model per §4.1, live-turn only. |
| FR-006 | MUST render only in `omnipus-pdfium`, sandboxed, no files or network. |
| FR-007 | MUST run LibreOffice only as a separate per-conversion child. |
| FR-008 | MUST locate and verify components per AD-4 without fallback. |
| FR-009 | MUST start the converter with AD-7 settings and exactly the AD-7 environment for its platform. Linux: `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`, `LANG=en_US.UTF-8`, scratch `HOME`, scratch `TMPDIR`, and no `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` in this landing (§3.6); only with §3.5 D8e (#1856) the call's `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`, except where D8e.2 leaves no private folder (no active kernel sandbox, folder not made). macOS: the first four of those, the call's `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`, and `URE_MORE_SERVICES` only when the verified registry folder is present. Windows: the first four, scratch `TEMP`, `TMP` and `USERPROFILE`, and the gateway's `SystemRoot`. MUST start the renderer and text extractor with an empty environment at construction; on Windows Go then adds `SYSTEMROOT` from the gateway. MUST NOT add any other variable. |
| FR-010 | MUST apply AD-7 sandboxing, else run unconfined with disclosure. |
| FR-011 | MUST report conversion validity, model delivery and thumbnails as separate outcomes per §4.3; ignore exit codes. |
| FR-012 | MUST enforce AD-6 limits, admission and Stop. |
| FR-013 | MUST clean scratch and recover per AD-8. |
| FR-014 | MUST return MV-1 messages. |
| FR-015 | MUST keep PDF preview without LibreOffice and degrade per AD-15 without the renderer. |
| FR-016 | MUST register the tool with AD-13 policies. |
| FR-017 | MUST apply §4.1 to uploads, incl. pages 1–10 + text + paging note, text fallback on failure. *2026-11-19, §3.7 G12.1:* an upload larger than the native limit, sent to a model that reads PDFs natively, MUST get the extracted text, not page images. |
| FR-018 | MUST decide PDF capability from the catalog only; delete the list; Haiku exclusion in the catalog. |
| FR-019 | MUST apply §4.1 to `read_file` on a PDF without offset/length. *2026-11-19, §3.7 G12.3:* only for a PDF whose text can be extracted; for a PDF with no extractable text `read_file` offers no native or image form (deferred to #1918; re-opened at the 2026-11-22 12:00Z checkpoint only if the fix round has spare capacity). |
| FR-020 | MUST store and deliver thumbnails per §5.1, web chat only. |
| FR-021 | MUST implement §5.3–5.5. |
| FR-022 | MUST cache rendered pages per AD-9. |
| FR-023 | MUST replace the four skills with `elicify-office`. |
| FR-024 | MUST ship components per §10; no runtime download. *2026-11-19, §3.7 PK:* on every documented install route, darwin/amd64 included; as separate release downloads listed in `checksums.txt`, fetched and verified by the installer by default, with `--without-office` and `--without-pdf-renderer`; deb/rpm carry both until #1912. |
| FR-025 | MUST delete the registry store after replacing the readiness gate. |
| FR-026 | MUST include license notices. |
| FR-027 | MUST update §17 docs in the same change. |
| FR-028 | MUST open `SKILL.md` with the AD-14 table. |
| FR-029 | MUST run create/edit recipes end to end in the real runtime. |
| FR-030 | MUST re-hash the renderer before every launch. |
| FR-031 | MUST enforce AD-6 retained-byte budgets with byte eviction. |
| FR-032 | MUST run all PDF parsing this feature adds in a killable program under AD-6 limits. |
| FR-033 | MUST implement protocol v1 and validate before allocating. |
| FR-034 | MUST implement the per-platform launcher ownership of AD-2. |
| FR-035 | MUST implement the §5.2 contract across all producers. |
| FR-036 | MUST keep thumbnails out of model media. |
| FR-037 | MUST implement §5.3 states and §5.4 focus behaviour. |
| FR-038 | MUST provide the Retry route of §5.2. |
| FR-039 | MUST serialize native PDFs per §4.2 for every retained adapter and entry point; withhold only for documented upstream limits via the catalog. |
| FR-040 | MUST re-authorize sources before each provider send. |
| FR-041 | MUST implement every §4.3 row. |
| FR-042 | MUST provide `omnipus office register` / `pdf-renderer register` / `unregister`. |
| FR-043 | MUST disable a component whose root any agent can write. |
| FR-044 | MUST materialize image-delivered uploads into the workspace for pagination. |
| FR-045 | MUST enforce the named Windows test inventory. |
| FR-046 | MUST test the four inspectors against their contracts with mutations; apply §6 dispositions. |
| FR-047 | MUST remove the document runtime's `soffice` contract. |
| FR-048 | MUST state the macOS best-effort memory boundary in UI text and docs, with no hard-cap claim. |
| FR-049 | MUST refuse, where it is created, any new write access that overlaps a preview program folder — shell approvals, mounts and configuration saves/reloads — with the §3.2 texts (founder S2 = A). |
| FR-050 | MUST resolve every post-start gateway file operation in a call's scratch folder through a handle opened before the first start, never reaching outside it (§3.1 D3.7, founder SR1). |
| FR-051 | MUST keep the owner record outside the converter's write reach wherever the converter has a file boundary; recovery MUST act only on that record and MUST derive the process group from the verified launcher, never from a stored number (§3.4 D1–D4, founder Q18). Where no file boundary exists the limit in §3.4 D7 applies (founder Q22). *Narrowed 2026-11-16 (§3.6 VC-2), Linux, this landing:* when the data folder lies under `/tmp`, the converter's remove right there reaches the owner record, which it can then delete but not forge or change; this is a documented limitation of this landing, tracked under #1856, and the MUST holds for a data folder outside `/tmp`. |
| FR-052 | MUST NOT start the converter again when its scratch folder was replaced; the call ends with the existing `conversion failed: scratch unavailable` text (§3.4 D6, founder Q23). |
| FR-053 | MUST answer a configuration refusal on every route of §3.4 D9 and D9b with the exact §3.2 text, as a 400, except where D9 or D9b states the route's own rule; writers with no answer to a person MUST log the text (founder Q24, Q26). |
| FR-054 | MUST let a preview started from an agent turn use its own scratch folder on a home where `tmp` exists, every time; the fix MUST open only `<id>/work` (§3.4 D8). |
| FR-055 | MUST confine the converter's reads to the §3.4 D8c list on every start and in both file models, never the calling turn's read rights; on macOS the verified filtered registry folder is read in place, read-only; the converter MUST NOT be able to read another call's folder, the owner record, a workspace or agent file, or any other part of the data folder (§3.4 D8c, founder Q35, Q36); looking up the folders above its own (§3.4 D8d) is the only other access. |
| FR-056 | MUST give the converter on macOS look-up-only access (existence and attributes; no listing, no reading, nothing below) to exactly the folders above the folders of its §3.4 D8c list, `/` excluded, and to nothing else beyond that list; no other policy MUST carry this access; the Linux backend MUST NOT turn it into any grant (§3.4 D8d; takes effect on founder Q37 = A). |
| FR-057 (deferred to #1856 — not part of this landing) | On Linux the converter MUST get, beyond the §3.4 D8c list, only read and write on its own per-call folder `/tmp/o-<id>` (founder Q-B A) and read on `/dev/urandom` (Q77 A); MUST NOT get any rule on `/tmp` itself or on `/var/tmp`; MUST run under the converter filter (F-1…F-6) (§3.5 D8e; founder Q78 C, Q80 A). Where the kernel sandbox is not active, FR-060 governs the filter and the folder (§3.5 D8e.2). |
| FR-058 (deferred to #1856 — not part of this landing) | The call's socket folder MUST be removed by its exact recorded path when the call ends and at startup recovery, in almost all cases; known limits: an unrecorded folder after a crash during creation (accepted, V2-F4 B) and reused-name recovery (tracked, T2); a failed removal MUST keep the record and stop before `work`; Omnipus MUST never list or sweep `/tmp` (§3.5 D8e.3). |
| FR-059 (deferred to #1856 — not part of this landing) | The **Office converter** MUST NOT be able to create an IPv4 or IPv6 socket itself and MUST NOT have port-0 or other port allow rules. Renderer and extractor rules unchanged (§3.5 D8e; founder Q79 A, Q-C B, V2-F2 B). Where the kernel sandbox is not active and the filter is not installed, FR-060 applies. |
| FR-060 (deferred to #1856 — not part of this landing) | With the kernel sandbox active: `/tmp` check, folder creation or filter installation failure MUST fail the preview with the D8e messages (§3.5). Without it (founder flag 2 = B): a folder failure MUST be logged, the converter MUST start with no `OSL_SOCKET_PATH` and no filter, and the preview runs as before under the existing disclosure; a filter failure MUST be logged and the preview runs as before under the existing disclosure (founder Q-A B). |
| FR-061 | On Linux, in this landing, the converter MUST get, beyond the §3.4 D8c list, exactly these rights and no others: make-socket and remove-file (and no other right) beneath `/tmp`; read, file read only, on `/dev/urandom`, `/etc/passwd` and `/etc/nsswitch.conf`. It MUST NOT get any other right on `/tmp`, any rule on `/var/tmp`, `OSL_SOCKET_PATH`, or any other new rule or setting; these rights MUST NOT be granted through `AccessWrite` and MUST NOT appear in the renderer's, the extractor's or any turn or tool policy; macOS and Windows MUST stay unchanged. **Accepted risk until #1856 (founder, 2026-11-16):** during a conversion the converter may create, remove and rename entries anywhere under the service user's `/tmp` (Landlock rules are hierarchical) (§3.6, founder Q1 A; replaced by FR-057…FR-060 when #1856 lands). |
| FR-062 | The user guide (`docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`, `docs/documents.md`) and the landing text MUST state plainly, for Linux: LibreOffice may create its control socket and remove or rename entries in the shared `/tmp`; it reads `/dev/urandom` and the local account list (`/etc/passwd`, `/etc/nsswitch.conf`); the stricter per-conversion folder and the converter network restriction are not switched on yet (#1856); a data folder placed under `/tmp` lies within that remove right (§3.6 VC-2). They MUST NOT claim any network protection for the preview programs on Linux, MUST NOT claim the converter is fully locked down or state anything that is false under §3.6; the §3.5 D8e user-guide text MUST NOT be published with this landing (§3.6 D5, §17). *Corrected 2026-11-21 (§3.9 F1):* the not-yet-switched-on part names only what #1856 adds, the converter system-call filter that refuses LibreOffice's own IPv4 and IPv6 sockets and the per-conversion private folder; the ban on any network-protection claim (VC-4) is unchanged |
| FR-063 | When a provider rejects a native PDF with a classified PDF-input rejection, the turn MUST retry with that document's extracted text in its place (the existing honest notice when extraction fails), for an uploaded PDF, a `read_file` result and a `preview_document` result alike, and MUST complete; the rejected PDF MUST NOT be sent again in that turn; page images MUST NOT be the recovery form; other error classes MUST NOT be turned into text retries (§3.7 G12.2; cloud chief ruling G12, 2026-11-19). |
| FR-064 | The release pipelines and the packaging dry run MUST run the release contents check, which MUST fail, naming each missing item, on missing components (a component download, its `checksums.txt` entry, its manifest or programs; a deb/rpm or Docker image without both components) and on a Chrome-for-Testing platform's main archive without the bundled Chrome (§3.7 PK7; #1913). |
| FR-065 | On Linux under the confined filesystem model, the gateway's startup rules (boot set) MUST carry the three BR-1 reads (`/etc/passwd`, `/usr/share/fonts`, `/usr/local/share/fonts`; read only, never execute) and, *amended 2026-11-20 (§3.8 BR-2; founder Q100 A)*, read and execute (`AccessRead \| AccessExecute`, never write) on exactly: (a) the real path of each packaged slot of LibreOffice and of the PDF renderer that exists when the startup rules are built (missing slots skipped); (b) the real path of the root named in each registered manifest that parses and exists; (c) the resolved omnipus program **file**, never its folder; and nothing more. Never granted: a parent folder of a slot, a `bin` folder, `/`, a restricted system path, or any entry inside or containing a folder the startup rules make writable (refused with a warning). These entries MUST be added only by `sandbox.DefaultBootPolicyForModel`, called only by the gateway startup; `sandbox.DefaultPolicyForModel` keeps its signature and delegates with none; `basePolicyForModel`, per-turn and tool policies, the open model, macOS and Windows MUST NOT carry any of them (§3.7 BR-1; §3.8 BR-2). *2026-11-21 (§3.9 OF-4):* on Linux, (b) only for a root strictly inside Omnipus's own folder (FR-068). |
| FR-066 | On Linux under the confined model with the kernel sandbox enforcing, a component whose folder was not granted at this start (installed, registered, moved or replaced in place since) MUST NOT be read or started, and the tool MUST report the §3.8 BR-2.5 texts; after a successful registration under the confined model on Linux, the registration commands MUST print the BR-2.7 restart line (2026-11-20, §3.8; founder Q100 A, team-lead rulings). |
| FR-067 | On Linux, in every filesystem model, `omnipus office register` and `omnipus pdf-renderer register` MUST accept a folder only when the folder as given and the root it would record, with every link resolved, both lie strictly inside Omnipus's own folder `dir(E)/../share/omnipus` (E = the resolved program file); otherwise they MUST refuse with the exact §3.9 OF-3 text, a non-zero exit, nothing written and no restart line, before anything inside the folder is examined. macOS and Windows registration MUST stay unchanged (2026-11-21, §3.9 OF-1…OF-3; founder OF). |
| FR-068 | On Linux, the resolver MUST refuse a registered manifest whose recorded root, as recorded or with every link resolved, is not strictly inside Omnipus's own folder, before `CheckStartupGrant` and before anything inside the root is examined, without falling back to a packaged copy; the tool result, `doctor` and the startup grant note MUST carry the exact §3.9 OF-4 texts; the PDF renderer keeps the reason code `renderer_damaged` (2026-11-21, §3.9 OF-4, OF-5). |
| FR-069 | On Linux, while the gateway's startup record is active, a component whose file cannot be read because access is refused MUST NOT be used, and the tool MUST report the exact §3.9 RS-1 texts; without an active record the existing damaged texts MUST stay (2026-11-21, §3.9 RS-1; architect gate A4). |
| FR-070 | When the provider's per-request PDF count is used up and a preview gets no page images, the model MUST receive the preview's extracted text with the exact §3.7 G5.6 note, never a "reads text only" reason; a preview without stored text MUST get the text extracted from its own PDF bytes; `read_file` MUST add no count note (2026-11-21, §3.7 G5.6; architect gate A5). |

| ID | Success criterion |
|---|---|
| SC-001 | Dev Mac, sandboxed, FX-PPTX4/DOCX6/XLSX3: p95 ≤ 10 s over 5 runs. |
| SC-002 | Renderer start to first page p95 ≤ 500 ms; ≤ 100 ms median per further page (S-1). |
| SC-003 | Omnipus binary size unchanged in class; renderer ≤ 15 MB per platform (S-1). |
| SC-004 | T-31 worst-path gateway peak ≤ the AD-6 sum for that scenario; typical 20-page preview reported (target ≤ 100 MiB, not a claim until measured). |
| SC-005 | Every §11 row passes. |
| SC-006 | T-13 leaves 0 folders, 0 processes. |
| SC-007 | macOS profile diff = 3 rules (+ network); Mach = 2. |
| SC-008 | UAT: planted defect detected and fixed in docx, xlsx, pptx on macOS. |
| SC-009 | `openai/gpt-6-luna` receives native PDF via upload, `read_file` and preview (outbound evidence). |
| SC-010 | T-72 passes on Mac, Linux, Windows with every T-75 mutation caught. |
| SC-011 | T-91 reports PASS (not "not a measurement") on the exact candidate commit (founder O8 = A). |

**S-1** (backend-lead, before GREEN): pdfium source/version/checksum and license inventory; reproducible build inputs; build ID + protocol version; linkage and runtime dependencies; final installed hash after any signing; quarantined clean-install launch on Apple silicon (no quarantine removal, no Gatekeeper disabling); timings, size, memory behaviour per OS incl. the macOS watchdog feasibility; non-embedded-font and form PDFs; musl availability (*lapsed 2026-11-19, §3.7 PK3: Debian slim, glibc*); LibreOffice address-space vs data limit on Linux. *2026-11-19, §3.7 PK2:* the quarantined clean-install launch also covers an Intel Mac. **S-2:** provider documentation for PDF in user content and tool results (§4.2). Missed budgets or unavailable targets → founder.

| `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md` "Document preview programs", sentence (1) of the D8c row (2026-11-08, §3.4 D8d; founder Q37 = A) | Drafted by backend-lead with the omnipus-voice skill; docs-verifier audits. Replace sentence (1) with: "When the kernel sandbox is active, LibreOffice can read only its own program files, the folder that holds the Omnipus program, the system libraries and fonts, and the temporary folder of the current preview. On macOS it can also read, but not change, the checked list of LibreOffice parts it may load, and it can check that the folders above these exist, without seeing what is in them. It cannot read your files, the agents' files, other previews or anything else Omnipus stores." Add after it: "Checking a folder shows when something in it was last added or removed, but not what. On Linux the kernel sandbox cannot stop any program from checking whether a file or folder exists; LibreOffice still cannot list folders or read files outside the folders named here." The other D8c sentences stay |
| `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md` "Document preview programs" (2026-11-15, §3.5 D8e; founder Q-A B, Q-B A, Q-C B, V2-F1…F4 B, flag 2 B, flag 3 A) | **Deferred to #1856 — not part of this landing; must not be published with it (§3.6, FR-062): several sentences below are false under §3.6 variant C (for example "LibreOffice cannot create or delete anything directly in `/tmp`").** Drafted by backend-lead with the omnipus-voice skill when #1856 is implemented; docs-verifier audits each sentence against the built behaviour. Insert after the paragraph that begins "When the kernel sandbox is active, LibreOffice can read only …": "**LibreOffice on Linux.** Each LibreOffice preview gets its own folder inside `/tmp`. Omnipus creates it so that only the account Omnipus runs as can open it, and LibreOffice keeps its control connection there instead of in the shared `/tmp`. During a normal conversion, other user accounts on the computer (except the administrator) cannot connect to it. A document crafted to take over LibreOffice could change that folder's permissions while the preview runs. This is a known limit that we plan to close. / LibreOffice cannot create or delete anything directly in `/tmp` or write to files there, and it never uses `/var/tmp`. It can read the system's random-number source (`/dev/urandom`) and nothing else in `/dev`. Inside its own folder it can create and delete files. Where `/tmp` is kept in memory, a document crafted to do so can use memory there until the preview ends, at most 120 seconds per preview. Omnipus does not limit the total size. / LibreOffice cannot open network connections itself. It can still talk to other programs on the same computer through local connection points, and such a program could make network connections for it. / Omnipus deletes the preview's folder when the preview ends, and in almost all cases after a crash, when Omnipus starts again. / **When this protection is not available.** If the kernel sandbox is active but Linux does not allow the system-call filter LibreOffice needs, the preview fails with `conversion failed: this Linux system does not allow the system-call filter Office preview needs`. If the kernel sandbox is not active either (some older kernels, or the sandbox is switched off), the preview still runs, and the result says `converted without sandbox: <reason>`. If, in addition, Omnipus cannot create LibreOffice's own folder in `/tmp` (the log shows `office preview: private folder unavailable`) or cannot install the filter (the log shows `office preview: system-call filter unavailable`), LibreOffice works as it would without Omnipus: its control connection sits in the shared `/tmp`, protected only by the default file permissions of the account Omnipus runs as, and it can open network connections, including listening for them." (" / " separates paragraphs.) **Scope rule (founder flag 3 = A):** every network sentence names **LibreOffice** only; nothing is said, in either direction, about the network rules of the PDF renderer or the text extractor until T3 is fixed; the page must not use wording such as "the preview programs cannot open network connections". docs-verifier checks this scope rule when auditing the drafted page |
| `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md` "Document preview programs" and `docs/documents.md` "Limits and isolation" (2026-11-16, §3.6 variant C; FR-062; founder Q1 A) | Drafted by backend-lead with the omnipus-voice skill; docs-verifier audits each sentence against the built behaviour and checks that no sentence claims the converter is fully locked down. **Required content, Linux:** LibreOffice may create its control socket in the shared `/tmp` and delete or rename entries there that the account Omnipus runs as may delete; it reads `/dev/urandom` and the local account list (`/etc/passwd`, `/etc/nsswitch.conf`); the stricter per-preview folder and the block on LibreOffice opening network connections itself are not switched on yet (#1856); a data folder placed under `/tmp` lies within that delete right, so keep it outside `/tmp` (§3.6 VC-2, FR-051 narrowed). **Corrections to existing text:** (1) the paragraph "When the kernel sandbox is active, LibreOffice can read only …" (D8c/D8d rows) gains, for Linux, the random-number source and the account list, so its "only" list stays true; (2) `docs/documents.md`'s "the programs can write at most their own temporary folder" gains the Linux `/tmp` exception; (3) **required user-doc correction (team-lead ruling 2026-11-16, §3.6 VC-4):** delete its sentence "on Linux they cannot open or accept TCP connections where the kernel supports that rule (UDP is not blocked)" — it is false for all three programs; do not narrow it to LibreOffice; the page makes no claim about network protection for any of the three programs on Linux in this landing. The implementing lead drafts the deletion and the surrounding sentence repair; docs-verifier audits it. **Proposed paragraph** (sandbox-limitations, after the "can read only" paragraph): "**LibreOffice on Linux.** LibreOffice keeps its control connection point in the shared `/tmp` folder. While it converts a document, it may create that connection point there, and it may delete or rename files in `/tmp` and its sub-folders that the account Omnipus runs as is allowed to delete. A document crafted to take over LibreOffice could use this during the preview to delete such files, including other programs' connection points, and to put its own connection point in their place. It cannot read or change what is inside files there. LibreOffice also reads the list of user accounts, so that its connection point belongs to the account Omnipus runs as instead of being open to every account on the computer. A stricter setup, with a separate private folder for each preview and a block on LibreOffice opening network connections itself, is not switched on yet; it is tracked in issue #1856." The landing text carries the same plain statement |
| 2026-11-19, §3.7 (G12, PK, BR-1) | Drafted by the implementing lead (backend-lead) with the omnipus-voice skill; docs-verifier audits each sentence against the built behaviour. **PK:** `docs/getting-started.md`: the installer fetches the Office preview components as separate downloads by default, their approximate sizes, `--without-office` and `--without-pdf-renderer`, a re-run with an opt-out removes that component, Intel Macs use the installer; `docs/docker.md`: Debian slim base, both components included; `docs/operations/platform-support.md`: darwin/amd64 released, Windows renderer zip for registration; `docs/documents.md` "What previews need": per route, and delete the note "Still to draft with packaging: the installer components"; deb/rpm users cannot leave Office out until #1912. **G12:** `docs/documents.md` "PDFs you attach to a message" keeps its text row for an oversized PDF (already correct) and gains one sentence that also covers `read_file`: when a provider refuses a PDF, the model gets its extracted text instead and the turn goes on. **BR-1:** `docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md` already names the three confined-boot reads and says the per-command sandbox does not include them (commit `c2be326a0`); docs-verifier audits that paragraph |
| 2026-11-20, §3.8 (BR-2; founder Q100 A) | Drafted by the implementing lead (backend-lead) with the omnipus-voice skill; docs-verifier audits each sentence against the built behaviour. **`docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`**, the paragraph that today says Omnipus's own sandbox on Linux "also allows three reads, and nothing more": rewrite it so it means: with `sandbox.filesystem_model` set to `confined`, Omnipus's own sandbox on Linux also allows the same three reads (the account list `/etc/passwd`, `/usr/share/fonts`, and `/usr/local/share/fonts` when it exists), and, so the preview programs can start, it lets the Office preview and PDF preview program folders that are installed when Omnipus starts be read and run, together with the Omnipus program file itself; nothing more: never writing to any of them, never the folders around them (for example not the whole `/usr/local/bin`), and the separate sandbox Omnipus builds for each agent shell command includes none of these. A preview program installed, registered, moved or reinstalled while Omnipus runs is used only after Omnipus is stopped and started (for example with the service manager); the in-app restart is not enough in the confined sandbox; until then a preview says so, and `omnipus office register` / `omnipus pdf-renderer register` print a reminder. The sentence must not claim the programs are readable by agents or that "nothing more" than the three reads is allowed. **`docs/documents.md`** "What previews need": one sentence for Linux with the confined file model: after installing or registering a preview program, stop and start Omnipus (the in-app restart is not enough); the exact tool text is `Office preview was installed or registered after Omnipus started. Stop and start Omnipus (for example with your service manager) to use it; the in-app restart is not enough in the confined sandbox.` |
| 2026-11-21, §3.9 (founder OF; gate A2/F2, A4, A5, F1) | Drafted by the implementing lead (backend-lead) with the omnipus-voice skill; docs-verifier audits each sentence against the built behaviour. **`docs/operations/sandbox-limitations.md`**, "Document preview programs": (1) replace "so agents can neither read nor run these programs" with the meaning of BR-2.4 as rewritten: the permissions are never added to an agent's sandbox, and agents cannot read or run the preview programs, except when Omnipus itself was installed into a folder agents can already read and run (`/opt`, `/usr/lib`, `/usr/lib64`, `/lib`, `/lib64`, `/usr/bin`; for example an installer run with `OMNIPUS_INSTALL_DIR=/opt/omnipus/bin`) or when you give agents that access yourself (`sandbox.allowed_exec_paths`, or an approval that lets a command read or run there); none of the standard installs is the first case; (2) the "Each folder registered" row: on Linux only a folder inside Omnipus's own folder (§3.9 OF-1 shipped values) can be registered; (3) the #1856 sentence: exactly the §3.9 F1 wording, no claim about network protection in force today (VC-4); (4) the in-app-restart paragraph: a program added after the first start and then hit by an in-app restart shows the RS-1 text. **`docs/documents.md`**: (1) "What previews need": on Linux, registration accepts only a folder inside Omnipus's own folder, with the per-route folder (OF-1 table), the exact OF-3 refusal text for LibreOffice, and what happens to a registration elsewhere made before this version (the OF-4 tool text, the `doctor` warning, `omnipus office unregister` or re-register inside the own folder); macOS and Windows unchanged; (2) the confined-Linux bullet: add the RS-1 text; (3) "Limits and isolation": the #1856 sentence replaced by the §3.9 F1 wording; (4) the 32 MiB paragraph's last sentence: past the per-request PDF count, a document the agent opened goes as page images or, when no page images go, as its text with a note naming the count limit (G5.6). **`docs/getting-started.md`**, the `--without-office` note: one sentence that on Linux a LibreOffice you install yourself can be registered only from a folder inside Omnipus's own folder. **`docs/docker.md`**: one sentence that a LibreOffice mounted into the container elsewhere than `/usr/local/share/omnipus` cannot be registered. **`docs/operations/platform-support.md`**: check only; its Windows registration text stays true. ## 20. Holdout evaluation (not in TDD or traceability)

1. Mia's one-page flyer opens in Word without overflow.
2. A 25-slide deck previewed "21-25" shows the last five slides, also in the chat.
3. A financial workbook preview shows totals matching a hand calculation.
4. A password-protected PDF yields one clear sentence, no images.
5. A deck linking a canary image produces no canary hit on macOS.
6. A 300-page PDF to an image-only model: page 5 from images, page 200 after paging.
7. Killing the gateway mid-preview, restarting and previewing again leaves no folders or processes.
8. A Claude 5.x or GPT-6-class model receives an uploaded PDF natively.
9. With the renderer removed, a native-PDF model still answers about page 3 and the person sees the unavailable card.

The architect's proposed BR-2.6 (a clean in-app restart) was vetoed by team-lead the same day; every restart text says "stop and start Omnipus", T-128 (e) is removed, and the in-app restart's inherited startup rules are a known limitation with a follow-up issue to be proposed by team-lead, not filed (§3.8 *Consequences*). 2026-11-21: founder decision OF applied (§1, §3.9 OF-1…OF-7, FR-067, FR-068), with the architect's scoping inside it: Linux only, every filesystem model, both components (§3.9 OF-2; the founder confirmed "Both programs (Recommended)", 2026-11-21, founder, terminal question tool, relayed by team-lead). Gate items A4 (RS-1), A5 (G5.6), A6 (ADR-024 correction) and docs-verifier F1 are folded in; F1 follows team-lead's VC-4 ruling and founder flag 3 A, so docs-verifier's sentence stating the TCP refusal is not adopted (§3.9 F1); team-lead accepted this the same day, and VC-4 and flag 3 A stand. Open for security-lead: §3.9 risk points 1–4. **OF-Q1 — close the two remaining exceptions of BR-2.4? Answered 2026-11-21, founder, terminal question tool, relayed by team-lead: "Document only (Recommended)" (A).** After OF, agents can still read and run a preview program in two cases: (i) Omnipus itself was installed into a folder every agent command can already read and run (`/opt`, `/usr/lib`, `/usr/lib64`, `/lib`, `/lib64`, `/usr/bin`; for example the installer with `OMNIPUS_INSTALL_DIR=/opt/omnipus/bin`); (ii) the operator lists the folder in `sandbox.allowed_exec_paths`, or a person approves a command's read or run access there. No standard install is case (i). Options put to the founder: **A (recommended, chosen): document both cases, build nothing more.** Simplest; both need a deliberate choice by the operator or a person, and the docs state them (§17). **B: refuse at start, on confined Linux, a packaged or registered program folder that lies inside one of those system folders or an `allowed_exec_paths` entry** (one more refusal reason next to BR-2.3's, with its own text). Closes (i) and the configuration half of (ii); an operator who gives agents `/usr/local` as an exec path, or installs Omnipus under `/opt`, then loses previews until that changes; approvals stay documented. **C: warn only** (startup log and `doctor`), no refusal. Result: both exceptions are documented (§17), nothing more is built. No founder question is open for this amendment.
